- `2026-08-07 | conventions | minor | no | memory-scope-defaults-to-project`: an agent in a different adopting project (Overture infra) recorded a per-project roadmap-formatting preference into the developer's own global profile instead of that project's memory, backwards for a fact whose entire premise is "some projects want this, others don't." `AGENTS.md`'s "Memory and contribution hygiene" (root and template) already stated the promotion direction (an insight that could apply everywhere gets offered for global promotion) but never stated the default scope for a brand-new fact before that decision. Added the test explicitly: default to project-scoped unless the fact is genuinely about the developer across every project, not this project's own nature. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-07 | conventions | minor | no | atlas-draft-staging`: a rudimentary first version of the long-parked volatile-facts-tier design question, now with a real confirming incident behind it (Arranger, 2026-08-07): live-investigation output, a depth count, a schema's specific field names, went straight into permanent `.dev/docs` content twice before the investigation actually settled, each requiring a multi-file surgical correction afterward. Added to `documentation.md` § The atlas: stage genuinely unverified investigation output as a draft in project memory space (`<project-memory-dir>/drafts/*.md`, never indexed in `MEMORY.md`), promote it into `.dev/docs/atlas/` once verified and genericized, replacing the draft. Scoped narrowly to what the incident actually showed, atlas-bound writes specifically, not a general mechanism for any devctx content; broader parts of the original design (generalizing past the atlas, letting a concurrent session discover a draft) stay open in `.dev/roadmap.md`. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | unattributed-content-scoped-to-git`: observed across more than one project: encountering a memory entry, atlas write-up, or other persisted artifact a different session wrote produced "someone changed this, wasn't us, will ignore it," a third, worse response than either trusting or validating it. `session-discipline.md` § "Unattributed working-tree changes" already required validating unattributed content rather than blindly trusting it, but its framing was scoped entirely to git's own working tree (`git status`/`git diff`), leaving memory and atlas content, which aren't git-tracked at all, without an obvious connection to the same rule. Generalized explicitly: the same discipline applies regardless of which persistence layer the surprise showed up in, and dismissing it isn't a valid alternative to validating it. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | cross-project-fallback-partial-infrastructure`: found while considering a project observed to have devctx but no project memory yet. `definition-of-done.md`'s cross-project fallback (surface to the developer if the other project has no devctx to write into) didn't cover the partial case, devctx present, but the specific layer a finding would naturally go in (memory, the atlas) doesn't exist there yet. Extended the fallback to cover this case too: surface it and let the developer decide whether to bootstrap the missing layer, rather than silently creating it or falling back to a worse-fitting one. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | template | minor | no | persistence-map`: the number of places a new fact or finding could belong grew to seven this week (project memory, global memory, `.dev/roadmap.md`, `.dev/tech-debt.md`, `.dev/docs/<service>/`, `.dev/docs/atlas/`, atlas drafts), each rule individually well-reasoned but scattered across several files with no single ordered path through them. Added `conventions/persistence-map.md`: a quick-reference index stating the order of questions and pointing at the file that owns each one, not re-deriving any of the reasoning. Dispatched from `AGENTS.md`'s "When to read what" (root and template) and listed in `template/README.md`'s file table. Written explicitly as a live, usable v1, not a finished taxonomy: expect revision as real cases test it. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | unsaved-buffer-not-visible-to-agent`: real, first-hand troubleshooting finding: an agent reading the filesystem directly (true of virtually every coding agent) can't see a file's unsaved editor content, an existing file reads stale, a brand-new unsaved buffer reads as not existing at all. Added a troubleshooting entry to `session-discipline.md` alongside the existing rename/multi-root one: the fix is saving the file first, or pasting its content directly into the conversation instead, and named this directly in a reply when it happens rather than only reporting the read failure. Also surfaced for human discoverability: neither this nor the existing rename/multi-root entry had ever been referenced anywhere a human would find them without already knowing to read `session-discipline.md`; added a "Troubleshooting" section to `template/DEVELOPMENT.md` pointing at both. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | cross-session-message-delivery-not-action`: a real, first-hand experiment sending cross-session messages to several live peers confirmed something worth generalizing: a successful send confirms the receiving session's inbox accepted delivery, nothing about whether that Claude actually saw or acted on it, governed entirely by the receiver's own inbound settings and permission mode, invisible to the sender. Extended `review-conduct.md` § "Ground truth over claims" (already covering external API writes like `gh api`) to cross-session messaging explicitly: report a send as delivered, not handled, absent a reply or an observed effect confirming otherwise. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | repo | minor | no | atlas-adopter-zero`: agentics' own first use of the atlas mechanism it shipped earlier today, on itself. Added `.dev/docs/atlas/interagent-communication-findings.md`, consolidating today's cross-session-messaging investigation (the on-disk registry, naming precision, delivery-vs-action, and a definitive cross-session test proving a session's sub-agents are never reachable from another session, only its main agent is) as prep material for a future "interagent communication" convention, plus `.dev/docs/atlas/index.md`, and cross-linked from the "Agent manager" roadmap item. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | context-efficiency-whole-session`: real usage data (`/status`) showed the bulk of one developer's spend came from sessions above 150k tokens of context, sessions active 8+ hours, and subagent-heavy sessions. `session-discipline.md`'s existing "On context efficiency" note only covered not re-reading unchanged files; extended it to the whole session: suggest compacting or a fresh thread at a natural task boundary, spawn a subagent because a task genuinely benefits from it, not by default, and use a lighter-weight model for a simple subagent task when the harness allows it. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | peer-introduction-stale-fact-unflagged`: a real, first-hand miss during today's self-introduction experiment: a peer session stated it was synced to agentics template 0.10.0, two releases and 21 unreleased entries behind, and this session didn't flag that as stale when replying, only noticing on a direct follow-up question. `AGENTS.md`'s existing "External content that overlaps with a project you maintain" bullet (root and template) already covers unprompted-connection cases for articles, documents, and conversations; extended it explicitly to a peer session's own message, since cross-session messaging is now a real channel this bullet's original wording didn't anticipate. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-08 | conventions | minor | no | peer-proposal-not-preauthorized`: a real incident while prototyping a family-registry design (see `.dev/docs/atlas/interagent-communication-findings.md`): a session asked to add itself to a file another peer had just created declined to act, reasoning that the file's creation was itself a structural addition to the developer's real global environment made without the developer's own review, not the peer's request to comply with, and that the requesting peer had already gotten one earlier detail wrong in the same exchange. The developer confirmed this was the correct call, not overcaution, and that the session which created the file in the first place should have checked in before writing into the live global environment rather than only describing the design. `AGENTS.md`'s "Check in before non-trivial decisions" (root and template) already covered exactly this shape of decision but had no language addressing a peer session's request as a possible, wrong, reason to skip it; extended it explicitly: a proposal from a peer session doesn't pre-authorize skipping the check, especially for anything with a lasting footprint outside the current project. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-10 | conventions | patch | no | interaction-parameters-accretion-bloat`: caught doing a live simplification pass on `AGENTS.md` (root and template) § Interaction parameters, prompted by a direct question about trimming context footprint: "Check in before non-trivial decisions" and "External content that overlaps with a project you maintain" had each grown a fully-restated clause the last time an incident extended them (129 and 93 words respectively), instead of folding the new case into the existing sentence. Tightened both to state the rule once, including the new case inline rather than as a second explanation, and added a missing `CHANGELOG.md` cross-reference to "Check in" that its sibling bullets already had. No behavior removed, only restated framing and a spelled-out example now carried by the citation instead. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-10 | repo | minor | no | simplification-pass-not-gated-on-publish`: found while running the fix above, `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Versioning's simplification pass only fired at the publish trigger, the exact shape `session-discipline.md`'s "Re-check before committing" already generalized away from for tech-debt/roadmap staleness (see `recheck-trigger-not-gated-on-commit`), just never applied to convention-prose density itself. A long stretch between releases let bullets accrete restated clauses with nothing to catch it until publish, which may be much later or never in the same working session. Added a standing self-check to `AGENTS.md` § Repo maintenance rules: run the same pass at each natural completion point, not only right before a release; the publish-time occurrence is now a backstop, not the sole trigger. `CONTRIBUTING.md` cross-references it rather than restating it. `breaking: no`

### 0.12.0 - 2026-08-05

- `2026-07-31 | conventions | minor | no | session-start-signal-silently-skipped-as-redundant`: a plain greeting stopped reliably re-triggering the session-start checklist for some agents, despite "Session-start signals" already saying to treat greetings as triggers "even mid-thread." Best-supported diagnosis: the rule never explicitly forecloses the reasoning "this feels redundant, we just did this," the same silent-exception failure `contributor-check-explicit-override` already named for a different mandatory check, and it's become more likely to fire as this file and `AGENTS.md` have both grown denser recently, diluting a plain greeting's salience against a larger rule set. Added an explicit clause: a signal fires every time it recurs, including immediately after the previous time, no judgment call. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-31 | conventions | minor | no | dashes-check-no-action-trigger`: `writing-style.md` stated the no-dashes rule and a fix recipe, but nothing told an agent to run the check as a discrete action immediately before output leaves its control. The gap was previously patched only as a personal refinement in one contributor's own global context, after a real incident (`virusseq-portal-ui` #522); it recurred anyway, surviving a single output but not a multi-step task (drafting several PR comments in a row), proving a personal file isn't enough and the rule needs a mechanical, testable action in the shared convention itself. Added: run `grep -c '—\|–'` against the exact final text immediately before send/post/commit, worded as a mandatory action, not a description. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-31 | conventions | minor | no | external-write-not-read-back`: `gh api -f body=@file` posted the literal string `@/tmp/pr181/c1.md` as a PR comment's body instead of the file's contents, since `-f` (raw-field) doesn't expand `@filename`, only `-F` (typed-field) does. The API returned a success URL for all 8 comments, and none were read back before reporting them posted; checking the first would have caught it before the other 7 went out broken. Added to `review-conduct.md` § "Ground truth over claims": a scripted or API-driven external write needs its actual result fetched and confirmed, not just its response code, plus verifying the first of a repeated batch before firing the rest. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-31 | conventions | minor | no | draft-doesnt-state-its-target`: a drafted PR reply was approved and posted correctly as an inline thread reply, but its closing line ("Approving now!") was written as though it also carried a formal review action; the PR's review decision stayed at Changes-requested, unaffected by the comment text, and the mismatch wasn't caught until the review decision was checked directly against the platform. `review-conduct.md`'s "Draft, never post" covered getting explicit approval on the text, but never asked what the draft actually corresponds to on the target platform, a reply to a specific thread versus a new top-level comment, or comment text versus a formal review action, both of which can be fully resolved in the wording while the platform-side effect stays ambiguous. Added: state both before presenting a draft, where it lands and whether posting it alone accomplishes the intended effect or a separate action is still needed. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | conventions | minor | no | upstream-check-missed-contradiction`: `convention-levels.md`'s upstream-update classification step only ever checked whether a new agentics entry *formalizes* something a project's own memory already tracked informally (duplication, prune the memory copy). It never checked whether a new entry instead *contradicts* something memory recorded as settled, a different failure needing a different response: surfaced to the developer as a real conflict, not silently resolved either way. Added that check alongside the existing one. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | repo | minor | no | no-ai-attribution-check-unenforced`: session-discipline.md's "No AI-tool attribution in commits or PRs" rule had no mechanical backstop, `check-consistency.sh` never actually scanned commit messages for it, despite this repo already applying the same "mechanical, checkable rule becomes a script gate" pattern to the dashes check. Added a check scanning commits ahead of the configured upstream (or just `HEAD` with none configured) for `Co-Authored-By`/`Generated with`/`Written by` style trailers. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | repo | minor | no | readme-illusion-of-context-intro`: `README.md` went straight from the pitch into "Quick start" with nothing framing why agentics matters in human terms first. Added "The illusion of context" as a new section between them: what a human means by an agent "understanding" a codebase, contrasted honestly with what that actually requires (context that survives past the session that created it, not comprehension agentics doesn't claim to give). `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | conventions | minor | no | external-content-overlap-unprompted`: an agent with no context on agentics was asked for a take on an unrelated article describing, in a different vocabulary, several of agentics' own actual mechanisms (persistent cross-session memory, codified team conventions, structured context handoff between tasks). It never named the overlap since it had nothing to connect it to, but the same gap applies whenever an agent that does have that context reads external material overlapping a project it maintains and doesn't say so unprompted. Added a bullet to `AGENTS.md`'s Interaction parameters (root and template, byte-identical) alongside the existing "surface ideas unprompted" one, naming this as its own recognizable trigger: externally-sourced content, not just internally-generated ideas. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | conventions | minor | no | generated-list-needs-feasibility-triage`: a ten-item feature-spec list was produced with every item stated at the same level of confidence, no signal for which were buildable now versus genuinely aspirational; only an explicit follow-up question surfaced the honest split. Added to `review-conduct.md` § "Push back, including on yourself": a generated list of options, features, or specs gets its feasibility triage before being presented, not only when asked which items are unrealistic. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | conventions | minor | no | non-mutational-scope-and-verified-exception`: `code-style.md`'s "Non-mutational" rule gave two examples (conditional object fill, loop accumulation) sharing an implicit target, accumulation as a substitute for computation, without ever stating that target; a cancellation flag recording an async event was flagged as a rule violation because nothing distinguished it from what the rule actually covers. Real incident showed the fallback itself was premature too: a fetcher wrapper's type signature was assumed to lack `AbortSignal` support, when the HTTP client underneath it (axios, already a dependency) supported `signal` natively the whole time, checked only after being prompted to, not before concluding a mutation exception was needed. Added the scoping clause to "Non-mutational", cross-referencing the existing capability-verification requirement, and a new paragraph to "Dependency version verification": a wrapper's current signature isn't proof the dependency underneath it lacks the capability either. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | conventions | minor | no | addressing-the-user-name-vs-label`: some agents referred to the developer as "the user" in conversational replies and visible reasoning even when their name was already known from global context, while others used the name directly, no consistency across sessions. Added "Addressing the user" to `writing-style.md`: use the known name when your agent's global context records one, fall back to direct second-person address ("you") when it doesn't, treat "the user" as a last resort rather than a neutral default. Scoped explicitly to live, ephemeral output only, persisted content keeps the existing "Name code, not people" rule unchanged. `breaking: no`
- `2026-08-05 | repo | patch | no | consistency-checks-doc-missing-new-check`: caught during this release's gap-scan, `testing/consistency-checks.md` still documented only 6 checks after `check-consistency.sh` gained a 7th (the AI-attribution commit scan) earlier in this same batch. Added the missing entry and renumbered the near-duplicate-entries check that followed it. `breaking: no`

### 0.11.0 - 2026-07-30

- `2026-07-30 | repo | patch | no | release-commit-subject-includes-version`: the release-process convention had drifted from the repo's actual recent git history: `CONTRIBUTING.md` said release commit subjects should avoid a `Release X.Y.Z` prefix, but the recent release pattern uses `Release X.Y.Z: <headline change>` for scanability. Updated the commit-message guidance to require that subject shape while keeping `CHANGELOG.md` as the canonical source for the version everywhere else. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | checking-in-scoped-to-code-style`: found while reducing duplication between a contributor's personal global context and this repo: `code-style.md`'s "Checking in before non-trivial decisions" was only ever reached via the "Writing code" dispatch trigger, but the rule applies to any non-trivial decision, design choices, memory pruning, roadmap changes, none of which are "writing code." A session doing purely docs or planning work could go the whole session without seeing it, another instance of `property-scoping-recurs`. Moved it into `AGENTS.md`'s "Interaction parameters" (root and template kept byte-identical, per the existing rule), read unconditionally every session, no dispatch trigger needed. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | template | minor | no | definition-of-done-file`: "Definition of done" existed only as a roadmap idea (six universal categories, five already covered individually by existing conventions, tied together by nothing) with no actual convention anywhere, and "is this done?" was already a recognized trigger phrase in `AGENTS.md` pointing at a checklist that didn't exist yet. Added `conventions/definition-of-done.md`: the universal checklist, a project-specific annex layer (agentics templates the pattern, not the checklist itself, per Arranger's `.env.schema` example), and a new seventh category, lessons learned persisted where they belong, defined in full since it's genuinely new. That category is a live, per-decision check (would a different session working on a different part of this project, or a project this one visibly affects, need this to avoid re-deriving it or contradicting a settled decision), reusing the same non-obvious-decision judgment already required for session-log bullets, routed to project memory (a pointer) or `.dev/docs` (the substance) depending on how much explanation it actually needs, and to a dependent project too when an already-known cross-project relationship makes the side effect visible. `session-discipline.md`'s "Refinement passes" is the enforcement backstop, not a separate mechanism: its three checkpoints now also ask whether a recognized lesson has actually been persisted yet. Wired into both `AGENTS.md` dispatch tables and the "Default review or audit posture" bullet (root and template kept byte-identical, per the existing rule), and `template/README.md`'s file table. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | template | minor | no | overture-docs-link-form-incomplete`: `CLAUDE.overture.md`'s docs-link rule only documented the GitHub-URL form, correct for content never published on the aggregator at all, but wrong for the more common case: a link to another Overture product's actual published docs page needs a `docs.overture.bio` URL, not GitHub, which would resolve to raw markdown source instead of the rendered page. Verified directly against `overture/docs`'s real `docusaurus.config.ts`, symlink script, and several product repos' actual `docs/` content before writing the fix, not assumed from the description alone. Restated the rule as three cases (relative within `docs/`, `docs.overture.bio` for another product's page, GitHub for anything never published on the aggregator), added real link breakage found while verifying (a broken same-folder link in Maestro, mismatched casing on what looks like the same cross-product target in two repos) as concrete review-time checks. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | memory-pruning-needs-different-cadence-per-role`: an audit of agentics' own project memory found four personal-project memories each explicitly noting they'd already been superseded by a shipped agentics convention, none ever pruned, plus two memories entirely misfiled under the wrong project. The existing roadmap intent treated pruning as one occasional-audit problem; a contributor actually creates this exact staleness in the same breath they ship the superseding convention, far more often than an ordinary adopter hits it via a template sync, so one audit cadence can't fit both. Split into two fixes: `convention-levels.md`'s upstream-update classification step now also checks whether a new entry duplicates something the project's own memory already tracks informally; `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Proposing changes gained a step to prune or trim the relevant memory immediately after shipping the convention that superseded it, not at a later audit. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | developer-identity-not-a-claim-to-verify`: surfaced while recording a developer's own GitHub handle in their global context so agents would recognize their PR comments as their own, not a third party's. `review-conduct.md`'s "Ground truth over claims" would otherwise apply uniformly, treating a PR comment the developer themselves wrote as an external claim needing independent corroboration rather than recognizing it carries the same authority as an instruction given directly in the conversation. Added an exception: content authored under an identity the developer's own global context records is treated with in-conversation authority; the ground-truth discipline still applies in full to what's being asked for, just not to whether the developer is the one asking. The specific identity value stays personal, recorded only in the developer's own global context, never in this repo. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | live-pointer-read-not-recached`: discovered while validating the propagation-boundary incident below: nothing said a live-pointer file needs re-reading each time its dispatch trigger fires within a session, only that it needs reading when the trigger first fires. An agent that read a conventions file early in a long session could keep reasoning from that read later, even after the file's actual content changed, e.g. a contributor's own concurrent session editing it mid-task, exactly the shape `long-thread-context-loss-signal` already names for a different cause. Added to `convention-levels.md` § "Leave as a live pointer": a trigger firing again means reading again, not reusing an earlier read, called out as mattering more for agentics specifically since it's an actively-edited live source. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | live-pointer-read-skips-remote-verification`: found in the same investigation: `convention-levels.md`'s remote-verification step (confirm the canonical URL is among a clone's configured remotes before trusting its freshness) only ever gated the session-start version-diff check, never the live-pointer read path itself. A stale or wrongly-sourced local clone, a contributor's personal fork with no `upstream` remote configured is the concrete instance found, could silently serve non-canonical content to every live-pointer read with nothing catching it. Tied the same verification to live-pointer reads, run once per session and cached rather than re-verified per read. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | propagation-crosses-repo-boundary-uninvited`: asked to "update your own agentics" from a different adopting project's session, an agent read that as licence to reach directly into the separate `softeng/agentics` clone and edit it live, mid-session, without being asked to touch the upstream side at all. Confirmed via file modification times: `review-conduct.md` was written at a timestamp this session never touched it, carrying two substantive additions, only one with a matching CHANGELOG entry. `convention-levels.md`'s "Agentics contributors" bullet said propagation was "always on without asking," true for surfacing a candidate, but ambiguous enough to also read as standing permission to execute the change in a different repository. The opposite failure from the entry below: that one made a deliberate propagation too expensive to trigger; this one made an unintended cross-repo write happen too easily. Reworded the bullet: surfacing the agentics repo as a candidate is always on, writing into it is a separate action that requires the current session to already be working inside that repo, or an explicit instruction naming that exact action. Default is now stated explicitly: fix the immediate issue in the current project's own copy first, then offer the agentics-side version back to the developer rather than reaching into that clone directly. Added a matching safeguard on this repo's own side too, in case an agent ends up here anyway: root `AGENTS.md`'s Critical Constraints now says not to modify any file here (not just `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`) without an explicit instruction naming this repo, and to check whether an ambiguous instruction more likely means "apply these conventions to your own current project" before assuming it means "edit the source here." `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | ground-truth-live-system-diagnosis`: an Arranger agent spent extensive effort tracing a CORS preflight failure through application code, three separate local reproductions, a Helm chart's Ingress template, and a live cluster's Ingress object annotations, all clean, before checking the actual deployed pod's environment variables directly, which immediately revealed a restrictive CORS allowlist as the real cause. `review-conduct.md`'s existing "Ground truth over claims" was scoped to code review only (a diff's prose, a commit message, CI status); the same discipline, verify the actual state directly rather than theorizing from what code could produce, applies just as much to diagnosing a live system, and wasn't stated as such. Added a paragraph extending the section to live runtime configuration. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-30 | conventions | minor | no | git-status-narrated-into-persisted-file`: an Arranger agent's session log entry stated "Everything above is unstaged in the working tree; nothing has been committed," restating the file's own current staging state as a persisted fact. `session-discipline.md`'s existing rule said to state git state plainly when reporting on changes, but never specified where, so it was applied to file content as readily as to a chat reply. The claim is true only until the next `git add`, after which the file keeps asserting something false with nothing revisiting it to correct it. Added a sentence to the Git section scoping that rule to conversational replies, and a matching concrete example to "Session file entry format," alongside "Write about effects, not style": the same underlying failure, transient process state narrated into a log whose job is durable facts. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-29 | conventions | patch | no | property-ordering-semantic-exception`: an Arranger agent proposed adding object/property key ordering to `code-style.md`, believing agentics had never encoded it; it already had, in `writing-style.md`'s "Property ordering" section (moved out of `code-style.md` the same day), the proposal's author hadn't checked existing coverage first. Two genuinely new refinements from the proposal were real, though: no carve-out existed for key order that's itself semantically meaningful (a reducer's accumulator, an enum-like ordered sequence), where alphabetizing would destroy something deliberate rather than just leave an arbitrary order unsorted; and conditional spreads (`...(condition ? { key } : {})`) mixed with plain keys are genuinely harder to eyeball for correct position than a flat literal, worth naming as a higher-risk spot for the drift the section already warns about, especially for output an external reader or system scans directly (an API response, a persisted structure). Folded both into the existing section rather than adding a new one. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-29 | conventions | minor | no | code-style-dispatch-scoped-too-narrow`: an Arranger agent's commit carried a `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailer despite the standing rule against it; asked why, it reported never having opened `code-style.md` at all that session, since its work was scoped to docs and tests, and the dispatch table only routes to `code-style.md` under "Writing code" or "Reviewing a PR or change." The rule applies to any commit regardless of what was touched, but the dispatch trigger only recognized code-writing: another instance of `property-scoping-recurs`, this time in the dispatch table itself rather than a convention's own prose. The same narrow gating affects several other `code-style.md` sections that are explicitly self-scoped beyond code (Dashes: "any output... without exception"; Language and typos: "in code, comments, and documentation"; Property ordering: "not just code or config"): a docs-only session could equally have missed any of them, and the developer pointed out the deeper issue is `code-style.md`'s own framing: a general user (docs, tickets) is still a git user, even though their role doesn't include code at all. Split the file: the universal sections (Dashes, Spelling and language convention, Language and typos, Property ordering) moved to a new `conventions/writing-style.md`, read unconditionally by "Starting a session" like `session-discipline.md` already is; the Git section (commit safety, no AI-tool attribution) folded directly into `session-discipline.md`, which already covers working-tree state and is read the same way. `code-style.md` now holds only genuinely code-specific conventions, correctly gated behind "Writing code." Root `AGENTS.md`'s own "When to read what" had the identical gap in a more extreme form: it never pointed at `code-style.md` at all, since agentics has no code to trigger "Writing code". It now points at the same two destinations, since agentics' own commits and prose are held to the same standard as an adopting project's. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-29 | repo | patch | no | personal-info-check-missed-untracked-files`: found while checking the fix below, `check-consistency.sh`'s personal-info scan only ever looked at `git diff --name-only HEAD` and staged files, which structurally excludes any brand-new untracked file. A `.dev/sessions/*.md` file is untracked from creation until first staged, and is exactly where a third party's name is most likely to leak (per the existing `third-party-name-precommit-catch` entry), so the automated check was silently blind to the highest-risk file type for its own job. Added `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard` to the scanned file list. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-29 | conventions | minor | no | unattributed-working-tree-changes`: a separate, concurrent session on the same local clone made unrequested edits to two files; the developer asked for them to be validated rather than the session having noticed and checked on its own. Added a `git status` check to "Starting a session" and a new "Unattributed working-tree changes" section to `session-discipline.md`: uncommitted content this session didn't make isn't evidence it's correct or reviewed, just that it's present. Validate it the same way any unverified claim gets validated (technical correctness, Critical Constraints, format/cross-reference consistency, duplication), report what was checked, and leave the keep-or-revert call to the developer rather than deciding it by how the content arrived. While already touching the file, fixed one pre-existing, unrelated em dash on the adjacent line being renumbered. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-28 | conventions | minor | no | monorepo-cross-package-test-blind-spots`: a downstream project's real, live-database integration test caught a bug that 30-plus passing unit tests had missed entirely: a fix to a workspace package (consumed elsewhere via a `file:../other-package` dependency) was correctly source-edited but never rebuilt, so the consumer app kept testing against stale compiled output: no error, no warning, only a real cross-package test surfaced it. Root cause distinct from the fix itself: unit tests inside the same package import from source via internal path aliases and structurally cannot cross that package boundary; only an import by package name, through the dependency's actual resolved entry point, exercises what a real consumer would run. In the same investigation, the project's own `test` npm script (`tsx --test ./src/**/*.test.ts`) turned out to silently skip test files at certain directory depths: `npm run test` executes through a non-interactive `sh`, which doesn't give the pattern the globstar (`**`) recursion an interactive shell would, so it behaves like a single `*` for one path segment: no error signals the gap, just a lower-than-expected test count. Added two sections to `testing.md`: rebuild `file:` dependencies (or write at least one real cross-package test) before trusting a "still passes" result after editing one, and prefer a bare `"test": "tsx --test"` (or `node --test`) with no path argument over a shell-glob pattern, since the runner's own recursive discovery isn't subject to the shell's globbing behaviour. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-28 | conventions | minor | no | refinement-passes-recurring`: the developer had to explicitly ask, twice in the same session, for an uncommitted-docs refinement pass against `documentation.md`'s rules; nothing in `session-discipline.md` triggered this on its own, so a long session's accumulated redundant or narrated prose across many files only got checked when asked for. Added a "Refinement passes: not just once at the end" section to `session-discipline.md`, requiring the same check semiregularly during a long session (after a natural cluster of edits, not after every edit), before committing anything, and before ending a session, cross-referenced against the existing pre-commit staleness check for `.dev/tech-debt.md` and `.dev/roadmap.md` rather than duplicating that trigger. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-28 | conventions | minor | no | rewrite-freely-until-committed`: session files kept accumulating chains of notes narrating their own edit history ("renamed to Y", then "renamed Y to Z", then "renamed Z to A") instead of one note stating the final fact. `session-discipline.md`'s existing "Collapse iteration to outcome" already named this failure, but scoped it to session files only. The real property is commit status, not file type: any uncommitted file is a draft, free to rewrite in place: another instance of `property-scoping-recurs`. A second pass, from another agent applying the same idea locally, found a deeper version of the same gap: even a single, unchained mention of a discarded value is noise if that value never existed in any committed or shipped state, since no reader could ever encounter it, not just chained mentions. Added a general "Rewrite freely until committed" section to `documentation.md` covering both depths, with a test ("would this stay true and useful to someone who only sees the current, merged state?") and a narrow exemption for artifacts whose entire job is recording change (a CHANGELOG entry, a migration guide, a PR description), where naming a discarded value is the point, not noise. Trimmed `session-discipline.md`'s section to point back at it, keeping only what's specific to session files: they close at day's end, a narrower boundary than "uncommitted," since their job is an honest same-day account. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-28 | conventions | patch | no | verify-conformance-external-content`: another agent's em dash slipped into a PR review comment despite the "no dashes" rule being read early in the session. Its own self-diagnosis found that `session-discipline.md`'s existing "Verifying conformance, not just structure" already names this exact failure mode (reading a rule isn't the same as checking against it at generation time), but scoped the fix to an enumerated list of internal governed artifacts, never extending it to ad hoc externally-published prose like a PR comment. Another instance of `property-scoping-recurs`: the principle's scope was stated by example rather than by the property that actually determines it (anything about to leave your control and become visible to someone else). Widened "Verifying conformance, not just structure" accordingly, with the dashes incident as the worked example. `review-conduct.md`'s "Draft, never post" now runs that check explicitly before presenting any draft for approval, since a human reviewer's attention is naturally on substance, not mechanics: the same reason the check didn't happen the first time. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-28 | conventions | minor | no | multi-root-workspace-breaks-agent-memory`: the developer hit a real incident in an unrelated (non-agentics) project: relocating three sibling repos under a shared parent and opening them as one multi-root VSCode workspace left Claude Code's chat panel empty, even though a repo in that set had a full, accumulated conversation history. Diagnosis found two genuinely different failures, not one. First, the workspace itself briefly failed to load at all (blank screen, no error) because its configuration still referenced a moved folder's old path: a separate, now-fixed blocker. Second, confirmed empirically afterward: Claude Code's persistent memory is keyed to a single resolved directory path, not to a repo's identity. This breaks two different ways depending on how a project is opened: a single-folder window can collide byte-for-byte with an old, differently-shaped path (a hyphenated name and that same name nested a level deeper both encode identically), while a multi-root `.code-workspace` window resolves every member repo to whichever one is listed *first* in the workspace file, regardless of active tab, so the other member repos' own histories are invisibly disconnected the moment the workspace is created. Rewrote `session-discipline.md` § Troubleshooting to state the underlying property once (path-based identity, not repo-based), with both manifestations under it. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-24 | conventions | minor | no | adversarial-audit-stance`: a colleague shared that Anthropic's own bun rewrite primes review prompts with "assume there are mistakes in this implementation," and the developer asked whether that biases too hard the other way. This maps onto something already validated this session: the developer's own "the ultimate question" is essentially the same technique, and it has found real gaps every time it's been invoked. `AGENTS.md`'s existing "Sanity check requests" bullet already required thorough *output* ("surface gaps... a shallow yes isn't an answer"), but never stated the adversarial *starting stance* that makes the search itself more thorough, plausibly why the softer wording alone needed a stronger, explicitly-named trigger to reliably work. Rather than add this narrowly to `review-conduct.md`, generalized it the same way `property-scoping-recurs` asked to: one new `AGENTS.md` Interaction-parameters bullet states the general posture, paired with a calibration against over-biasing (a search stance, not a quota; a finding must concretely matter to be worth surfacing). `review-conduct.md` and the developer's own "the ultimate question" memory now both point back at it as applications, rather than independent copies. The developer later confirmed a specific convergence: "is this done?" is literally this same question asked at the completion checkpoint, so the bullet now names it as a recognized trigger phrase alongside "does this look okay," the same way the still-undesigned "Definition of done" checklist item in `.dev/roadmap.md` will invoke this posture rather than needing its own separate mechanism once it exists. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-24 | repo | minor | no | root-template-real-content-diff`: `check-consistency.sh`'s "Root/template AGENTS.md section drift" check only ever compared bullet counts, which is exactly why it missed the "baked in" vs "baked into code" wording drift found earlier this session: same count, different text. Upgraded "Interaction parameters" to a real content diff, with the one known intentional difference (template's "agentics' CHANGELOG.md" pointer) normalized away first. Running the same real diff against "Critical constraints" showed that section is *meant* to diverge, not drift: root's content is repo-specific (agentics has no library code, but does need "we're the upstream source" awareness), while template's is generic-adopter (needs the environment-isolation rule a real project's library code needs). "Critical constraints" therefore keeps the looser count-based heads-up rather than a strict diff, which would just be permanent noise. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-24 | repo | minor | no | near-duplicate-regression-entries`: today's own `regression-checklist.md` had a real duplicate, two concurrent sessions each adding an entry for the same non-mutational loop-example fix, caught only by manual review. Added a word-overlap heuristic check (`python3`, heads-up only, never fails the script) flagging entries that likely describe the same incident. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-24 | conventions | patch | no | third-party-name-precommit-catch`: while writing up the incident above, the contributor's actual name landed in a session file entry twice, caught only on review before committing. The pre-commit consistency check couldn't have caught it: it only greps for the operator's own OS username, git identity, or fork name, all known in advance, and has no way to know whose name might appear while narrating something someone else reported. Added an explicit note to `session-discipline.md` § Name code, not people, and to `testing/consistency-checks.md`'s description of that check: this stays a judgment call at the point of writing, not something the script backstops. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-24 | conventions | patch | no | no-ai-attribution-overrides-default-template`: a contributor's agent added a `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailer to a commit despite `code-style.md`'s "No AI-tool attribution" rule, already read earlier that same session. Asked why: its own harness-level default commit template always appends that trailer, and it applied that default mechanically rather than checking it against the project's adopted convention at the point of writing the message. This is the same "read a rule, then generate content several steps later without re-checking it" gap `session-discipline.md`'s `verify-conformance-not-structure` already named, here as a competing external default rather than an internal structure/prose split. Added an explicit clause: this rule overrides the agent's own default commit-message template specifically, and reading it earlier in the session doesn't substitute for checking the actual message against it at the point of writing it. `breaking: no`

- `2026-07-24 | conventions | minor | no | verify-remote-not-just-diff`: a new agentics contributor's local clone was badly out of date, and his local agents kept trusting it instead of checking online. Diagnosis: the existing freshness check (`git log @{u}..`) only compares local against whatever remote the clone happens to track. If that tracked remote is itself a stale personal fork or an unintended mirror, the check truthfully reports "0 behind" while being badly out of date relative to the real canonical source: a false all-clear rather than a missed check. Separately found: the developer's own `projects.md` still marked agentics "sole-maintained, no freshness check needed", now false with a second contributor: exactly the kind of self-declared exception that silently outlives the fact that made it true. Added a remote-URL verification step to `convention-levels.md` § Checking for upstream updates and the matching `global-context/projects.md` note: confirm the clone's configured remote(s) actually include the documented canonical URL before trusting any local-vs-remote diff. Don't skip this for a believed-sole-maintained repo: the check is one cheap `git fetch`, and it's the only thing that would notice that belief stopped being true. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-24 | conventions | minor | no | contributor-stale-clone-notify-defer`: the developer wanted a related but distinct behaviour: when an agentics contributor's own local clone (not the project being checked) turns out stale, notify them, but don't switch into fixing it inline. Fixing it inline risks derailing a session that was never about agentics maintenance. Added to `convention-levels.md`: flag it plainly once, then record a reminder in the contributor's own global context or memory to offer fixing it next time that's convenient, the same way a deferred tech-debt item waits rather than being force-fixed on discovery. Scoped exclusively to agentics contributors: an opt-in `propagation_suggestions` adopter doesn't necessarily keep a local agentics clone at all. `breaking: no`


### 0.10.0 - 2026-07-24

- `2026-07-24 | conventions | minor | no | long-thread-context-loss-signal`: the developer noticed a greeting that used to trigger the full session-start checklist in a long-running session had stopped doing so. Diagnosis: whatever the agent's own mechanism for handling long threads (for Claude Code, an automatic compaction event), it most likely dropped the literal "Session-start signals" trigger phrases from context, since what survives is a narrative of what happened, not the exact instruction text read once, early, as file content. A later greeting then reads as a plain greeting, nothing wrong with the agent's behaviour, the rule that would make it meaningful just isn't there anymore. First draft of this fix named "compaction" specifically, which the developer caught as Claude-Code-specific terminology, violating agentics' own agent-neutral design principle applied to a mechanism rather than a path; reworded to the general phenomenon with a parenthetical for Claude Code. Added it as its own session-start signal to `session-discipline.md`, with an explicit note that it's structurally different from the phrase-based triggers: noticing it happened is the signal, don't wait for a later phrase to also match, since phrase-matching is exactly what this risks losing. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-24 | repo | minor | no | property-scoping-recurs`: the developer asked me to surface the pattern above unprompted next time, which prompted noticing it wasn't actually the first time: `Property ordering` needed widening from config files to code to markdown documents across separate passes, and `surface-unprompted-too-narrow` was the same shape applied to a different bullet, before this session's agent-neutral scoping bug made four. Added a `CONTRIBUTING.md` design principle, written for a human contributor too, not just an agent: state a convention around the property that actually varies, with concrete cases as examples, not the rule's real boundary. Separately, the developer wanted unprompted proactive surfacing like this reinforced generally, not narrowly re-stated on the already-twice-trimmed `AGENTS.md` bullet that already covers it: added a gap-scan step to `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s publish checklist instead, making a brief unprompted "what's missing" pass a standing part of every release rather than something that only happens when directly asked. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-24 | conventions | patch | no | non-mutational-loop-example`: an agent working in a downstream project wrote a `while` loop reassigning a local across iterations to walk up a directory tree, despite `code-style.md`'s "Non-mutational" rule already being in context. The rule's only example covered conditional object construction; the loop/accumulator shape wasn't illustrated, so the same violation wasn't obviously recognizable against it. Added a second example to `code-style.md` § Non-mutational showing the loop-with-reassignment shape and its recursive equivalent, matching the actual mistake rather than a generic reminder. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-22 | template | minor | no | agentic-projects-list-and-markdown-alphabetize`: while adding entries to the developer's personal cross-project map for the projects just synced to `CLAUDE.overture.md`, a version+sync-state note per project was rejected as noise (it goes stale the moment any one project syncs again, and the durable fact is already canonical in each project's own `AGENTS.md` tag), then a per-project "yes" flag was also rejected once every project in the set turned out to share it: the useful fact is a single consolidated list, stated once. Separately, the developer asked why the map's project entries weren't alphabetized at all despite `code-style.md` § Property ordering: the rule's own wording only ever named config files, code structures, and Terraform blocks, never markdown reference documents with named per-item sections, so a session reading it had no reason to apply it here. Added `code-style.md` § Property ordering: markdown reference documents with named per-item sections (a project map's headings, a glossary) now fall under the same rule, same reasoning as config keys. Added `template/global-context/projects.md` § Agentic projects: a single list of adopted-project names, explicitly scoped to update only on adopt/drop, not on every sync, so this doesn't quietly regress into the same per-item staleness problem. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-22 | conventions | minor | no | generalize-agentics-overrides`: `.dev/agentics-overrides.md` was only ever documented as something `upgrading-adoption.md` § 2 writes to, when a conflict found during a reconciliation pass gets permanently kept. The developer liked the pattern (recorded during today's `infra` sync) and asked whether it should be standard behavior more broadly: it should, since the same need, a durable record of "we decided against the convention's default, on purpose, here's why", comes up any time a suggestion gets permanently declined during ordinary session work, not only when the periodic upgrade-check happens to surface it. Moved the canonical definition and format to `session-discipline.md` § Recording a permanent override (read every session, not on-demand only); `upgrading-adoption.md` § 2 and `template/README.md` now point at it as one trigger among others rather than defining it independently. `breaking: no`

Root cause: `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s publish checklist never forced the check, it was left as "add one here when you fix that kind of gap" in the checklist file's own header, the same fragile-memory pattern the whole `deterministic-by-design` effort exists to eliminate, just not yet applied to this file. Backfilled the five qualifying entries; added a forced step to `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s publish trigger checking every `## Unreleased` entry against the file's own criteria before it ships. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-23 | conventions | minor | no | no-ai-attribution-in-commits`: the developer noticed agents adding "Co-Authored-By"-style trailers naming an AI tool to commits and PRs; harmless in intent, but it reads as the project endorsing a specific commercial product, not something to do on any vendor's behalf, paid or not, regardless of which agent is doing the work. Added `code-style.md` § Git: no AI-tool or vendor attribution in commit messages, PR descriptions, or PR comments. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-23 | conventions | patch | no | synced-sha-unresolvable-troubleshooting`: applying the rule above retroactively meant rewriting two already-published commits (one a release commit), locally, deliberately left unpushed until the next release folds it in. The developer noted this exact situation, a `synced:` SHA a project references ceasing to exist after agentics rewrites its own history, could recur for other reasons and asked for it to be documented rather than handled ad hoc again. Added `upgrading-adoption.md` § Troubleshooting an unresolvable `synced` SHA: distinguishes "the diff is empty" (nothing changed) from "the diff command errors" (the baseline itself is gone), and says to treat the latter like a missing tag, full § 1 diagnosis, not like "nothing changed." `convention-levels.md`'s diff step now points there instead of leaving the failure mode unhandled. `breaking: no`

Also fixed a gap this surfaced in the upgrade path itself: `AGENTS.md` § Initialization only runs for projects with no memory yet, so an already-adopted project would never get asked a question introduced after its initial setup. `upgrading-adoption.md` § 1 now checks every current initialization question against project memory and asks anything unanswered as part of the same non-conflicting batch. `CLAUDE.overture.md` also makes the cross-project map expected rather than merely offered for Overture projects specifically, since the ecosystem is inherently multi-repo, ahead of the generic "if the user works across multiple projects" trigger `AGENTS.md` § Initialization otherwise relies on. `breaking: no`

Added an explicit checkpoint to `session-discipline.md` § Keeping `.dev/` current: before committing anything that touches `.dev/tech-debt.md` or `.dev/roadmap.md`, or code covered by an entry added earlier in the same session, re-verify every entry added since the session began against current code state, rather than waiting for the next session's staleness pass. `breaking: no`

The developer suspected this recurs across `.dev/roadmap.md`, `tech-debt.md`, and session logs generally, and asked whether the fix belonged in "how to write" guidance or a forced checkpoint; given the exact lesson of `tech-debt-recheck-before-commit` above, trusting a writing-time ideal to hold across a long session is the same fragile shape, so it was folded into the same pre-commit re-check instead of left as a separate thing to remember. Added `session-discipline.md` § Say it once, at the density it deserves, and pointed `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s existing `succinct-wording-is-a-separate-pass` design principle at it as the convention-writing instance of the same rule, rather than keeping two independent statements of it. `breaking: no`

  - **Root vs. `template/AGENTS.md`: "Interaction parameters" wording had drifted** (template's phrasing was more refined and never back-ported) and **root was missing "Name code, not people"** in Critical constraints despite the same rule applying to this repo's own `.dev/` content; **`template/AGENTS.md` was missing the "grep before committing" step** root has. All three reconciled; confirmed via `check-consistency.sh`'s section-drift check, which now reports both sections at parity
  - **`security.md`/`security-guidelines.md`: the Quick threat model block and credentials phrasing were copy-pasted rather than cross-linked**, and `security.md:15`'s relative path ("copy `global-context/security-guidelines.md`") only resolved correctly from one specific, unstated base directory. Trimmed `security.md`'s Quick threat model to a pointer at the fuller version in `security-guidelines.md`; fixed the path to name the full location explicitly
  - **Two near-identical `security-guidelines.md` files** (`conventions/` and `global-context/`, 166 lines each) with no documented relationship: added a header note declaring `conventions/security-guidelines.md` canonical and `global-context/`'s copy a portable bootstrap copy that must be kept in sync, and synced their one substantive difference (a stale pre-`verify-dated-versioned-facts` OWASP line) so they actually match now
  - **`documentation.md:29`** referenced "an existing convention" without naming it: now names `session-discipline.md` § Session file entry format
  - **`testing/`'s own files violated the `terse-conventions-cite-changelog` principle they were built alongside**: the `global-guideline-material-never-in-project` incident got retold in full in `README.md`, `fixtures.md`, and `cold-read-review.md` instead of being cross-referenced once from `regression-checklist.md`. Fixed in all three. Also fixed in the same pass: `consistency-checks.md` cited a `CONTRIBUTING.md` step number that no longer matched after the step-collapse above; `check-consistency.sh`'s agent-neutrality check was silently scoped to `*.md` files only, narrower than what it claimed to check; its orphaned-file check matched a filename anywhere in `AGENTS.md` rather than specifically within the dispatch table; `fixtures.md` didn't say a dry run is more reliable run by a fresh-context agent for wording changes, and its "Running one" steps didn't name `session-discipline.md`/`upgrading-adoption.md` or explain how to point a check at a scratch directory
  
  `breaking: no` throughout: every fix is either a consolidation (content unchanged, second copy removed) or a clarification; no adopter-facing file shape changed
- `2026-07-21 | conventions | minor | no | dev-content-not-just-format`: checking Lyric and Maestro's `.dev/` files for the bootstrap-artifacts incident (`global-guideline-material-never-in-project`) surfaced three more content problems `upgrading-adoption.md`'s diagnosis never looked for, because it only ever checked file *shape* (tag, dispatch table, session-file format), never entry *content*: two individuals named directly in Lyric's `roadmap.md`, and a duplicated tech-debt-format instructional comment added to both projects' `tech-debt.md`, restating what `session-discipline.md` § Tech-debt entry format already defines, drifting from it the moment either copy changes. Fixed at the source: `session-discipline.md` § Keeping `.dev/` current now states that `roadmap.md`/`tech-debt.md` hold concrete decisions and issues, not process or event narrative (who raised something, in which PR), the same reasoning already applied to session files, and explicitly ties this to where names sneak in; § Tech-debt entry format now says not to restate itself inside a project's own file, reference it by name instead. `upgrading-adoption.md` § 1 adds a diagnosis item scanning existing `.dev/` entries for exactly these three problems, not just checking the files exist in the right shape; § 2 treats fixing them as non-conflicting, batched with other fixes rather than asked about individually, since there's no legitimate reason to keep a name, a restated format comment, or process narrative in place of the decision. `breaking: no`: no adopter file shape changes, this only sharpens what the diagnosis looks for
- `2026-07-21 | repo | minor | no | testing-agentics`: agentics has no compiler or test runner, it's prose, and the `global-guideline-material-never-in-project` incident (above) shipped without being dry-run against a real project first, going straight from plan-mode approval to live. Added `testing/` at the repo root, deliberately outside `template/`, contributor-only, never copied into an adopting project: `README.md` (index, when to use which practice), `fixtures.md` (four disposable synthetic-project shapes to dry-run dispatch-table or verbatim-copied-procedure changes against, so a real project is never the test subject again), `regression-checklist.md` (a curated, living list of past behavioral/interpretive incidents from `CHANGELOG.md`, each restated as a concrete scenario to re-verify, not a full history), `cold-read-review.md` (spawn a fresh-context agent to apply a change literally, since the author's own context hides exactly the ambiguity a first-time reader hits), and `scripts/check-consistency.sh` plus `consistency-checks.md` (a real, runnable script consolidating this repo's existing ad hoc greps: agent-neutrality, orphaned convention files, personal-info leaks, and a root/template `AGENTS.md` section-drift heads-up). Wired into `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Proposing changes (steps 8-9) and root `AGENTS.md`'s dispatch table.
