Added it. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | repo | patch | no | publish-simplification-pass`: added a simplification pass to `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Versioning's "Publishing a release," immediately after the existing staleness pass: before confirming the rollup bump, check whether anything about to ship can be simplified, de-duplicated, or replaced with a pointer to something that already exists, rather than a new part restating it, then re-run the matching `testing/regression-checklist.md` scenario if the simplification touches a section one exists for. A standing practice at every release now, not the one-time retroactive trim `terse-conventions-cite-changelog` already did once. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | repo | patch | yes | global-guideline-material-never-in-project`: running the upgrade check against two live adopters (Lyric, an upgrade; Maestro, a fresh bootstrap) both created local `conventions/`, `CLAUDE.roles/`, and `CLAUDE.softeng.md` copies in the project itself. `AGENTS.md`'s dispatch table uses bare relative paths (`conventions/code-style.md`) with nothing marking them as pointers into agentics rather than local paths, ambiguous once copied verbatim into a project's own `AGENTS.md`; separately, `template/README.md` treated copying these into a project as conditionally acceptable ("if your global context doesn't already cover it"), when the correct model has no such condition: this content is global-guideline material, it never belongs in a project, full stop. If a contributor's global context doesn't yet define a role or team conventions, the fix is bootstrapping their global context once, the same way `global-context/` templates already get copied to `~/.claude/` (or equivalent), not placing a copy inside whatever project they're adopting agentics into. Fixed: `template/README.md`'s "How to adopt" no longer lists `CLAUDE.softeng.md` as a per-project copy step, replaced with a one-time global-context bootstrap step; its bootstrap-artifacts note and "already have an existing setup" section both drop the conditional exception. `upgrading-adoption.md` § 1 states plainly that these three are never a gap to fix in the project, regardless of global-context coverage. Both `AGENTS.md`'s (root and template) "When to read what" mark which paths are live pointers into agentics versus local copies. `breaking: yes`: any adopter who already ran the upgrade against the earlier, ambiguous version may have the same wrongly-created local copies and needs to remove them manually; the fix prevents new occurrences, not backfill existing ones

### 0.5.0 - 2026-07-21

- `2026-07-21 | conventions | minor | no | upgrade-diagnosis-global-context-scope`: worked through a real scenario, an opt-in (non-contributor) adopter picking up the `propagation-suggestions-global-scope` fix via an explicit "update agentics" request, and found `upgrading-adoption.md`'s diagnosis and apply steps are scoped entirely to project files (tag, session format, `AGENTS.md` completeness, agent-neutrality); nothing in either step recognizes an entry describing a fix to the *reading agent's own global context* rather than a file in the project. Such an entry would get surfaced as changelog text but risk never actually being applied, since nothing tells the procedure to check or correct the agent's own persistent settings the way it already does for project files. Added a bullet to § 1 naming this category explicitly while reading new entries, and a bullet to § 2 requiring the correction be checked and confirmed, not just narrated, before treating the entry as resolved. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | conventions | patch | yes | propagation-suggestions-global-scope`: `template/AGENTS.md`'s initialization asked for `propagation_suggestions` and recorded it in project memory only, while `session-discipline.md`'s session-start check reads it from global context, a direct contradiction that meant an opt-in adopter's preference never actually carried across their own projects, exactly the gap `agentics_contributor` was already fixed for, just never mirrored onto this flag. Fixed: `propagation_suggestions` is now recorded in global context as the default during initialization, the same "record in global context, not just project memory" treatment `agentics_contributor` already has, with a project's own memory able to locally override that default (mirrors the existing `agentics_upstream_check: no` per-project-or-global override shape). Updated `template/AGENTS.md`'s initialization step and `convention-levels.md`'s Propagation suggestions and Checking for upstream updates sections. `breaking: yes`: an adopter whose `propagation_suggestions: yes` currently sits only in one project's memory needs to re-record it in global context for it to actually apply elsewhere, same as the earlier `agentics_contributor` fix required

### 0.4.0 - 2026-07-21

- `2026-07-21 | repo | minor | no | publish-precommit-staleness-pass`: the staleness pass `session-discipline.md` already calls for at session start (mark completed roadmap items done, close resolved tech-debt entries) had no equivalent at publish time, so `.dev/roadmap.md`/`.dev/tech-debt.md` could drift from reality across several releases before the next session happened to catch it. Added it as a precommit step in `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Versioning's "Publishing a release": run the same pass immediately before confirming the rollup bump. Applied once immediately: removed a fully-resolved bullet (the brace-rule proposal, already implemented and separately logged) that was still sitting in the roadmap's "not yet agreed" list with a note saying it wasn't actually open, exactly the kind of drift this step exists to catch. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | conventions | patch | no | review-purpose-ask-phrasing`: step 1 of `code-review.md`'s pre-review gate said to ask when the problem isn't stated, but not how to phrase that ask well. Added a worked example: propose your own best-guess interpretation of the intent and ask the author to confirm or correct it, rather than an open "what's this for?" Easier for the author to react to, and it exposes your own possibly-wrong read to correction, tying back to `review-conduct.md`'s "push back, including on yourself." `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | conventions | minor | no | review-goal-mismatch`: `code-review.md`'s pre-review gate asked whether the problem was stated, but never asked whether the diff actually solves it, a change can be well-written, correctly placed, and still address a different, related, or easier problem than the one named. Added as step 2 (renumbering the rest): does the change actually solve the stated problem, not just something adjacent to it. Explicitly the review-side application of the existing "verify purpose alignment before implementing" Interaction parameter, cross-referenced rather than duplicated. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | conventions | minor | no | review-conduct`: five separate agents were each asked to extract the review pattern from their own recent work (a ticket investigation plus public thread, a live multi-session PR review, two real PR reviews, a design/spec review, a PR closeout verifying prior feedback landed); workshopped the results together rather than adopting any one wholesale, stripping project-specific detail and anything that was really a code-style concern (already `code-style.md`'s territory), and sorting what remained by behavior rather than by which phase (ticket, live PR, closeout) it came from, since almost none of it turned out to be phase-specific. Added `conventions/review-conduct.md`: check existing coverage before contributing, ground truth over claims (including your own prior claims in the same review), ask only at real forks, push back including on yourself, every finding gets a disposition, disclose what wasn't verified, draft never post, respect the human's stated scope and signals. Deliberately a sibling to `conventions/code-review.md`, not a superset: that file is the pre-review "does this belong at all" gate; this one is how to conduct yourself once actually doing review work, ticket, live PR, or closeout alike. Wired into `template/AGENTS.md`'s dispatch table and `template/README.md`'s file list, cross-linked from `code-review.md`. Narrowed the agentics roadmap's "PR review workflow" item to what's still actually open: governance (approvals, branch protection, SLAs), a distinct concern from the methodology this entry covers. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | repo | patch | no | release-commit-message-style`: the first commit made under the new publish-trigger process described its own mechanics ("relocates today's Unreleased entries under 0.3.0") rather than what actually changed, used a relative day word ("today") that means nothing once committed, and titled itself "Release 0.3.0", restating a number `CHANGELOG.md` § Released already owns exclusively, when the version is internal machinery (the upstream-check's tag comparison) rather than something a contributor scanning `git log` needs. Amended it twice and rewrote `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Versioning's commit-message guidance: subject is a declarative summary of the headline change, not a version stamp; body is a verb-first list of the rest of what changed in substance, adds X, removes Y, improves Z, not the process of assembling the release, and no relative day words. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-21 | conventions | minor | no | property-ordering-covers-code`: a live session drafting TypeScript across two repos (types, destructured function parameters, object literals in test fixtures) drifted out of alphabetical order repeatedly; `code-style.md` § Property ordering only ever named "config files (YAML, JSON, etc.)", so the rule never fired for code even though the same drift risk (silent duplicate keys, inconsistent insertion points) applies there too. Extended § Property ordering to state explicitly that it covers object literals, `type`/`interface` fields, and destructured function parameters, not just config, while carving out plain positional call-site arguments as unaffected (their order is fixed by the signature). Added a "watch for drift across a multi-step task" note, since the miss wasn't a one-off, it recurred across many similar call sites once a shape got copy-pasted, and a mid-task field addition landed at the end instead of its alphabetical slot. Surfaced `eslint-plugin-perfectionist` (registry-verified at time of writing, autofixable `sort-objects`/`sort-interfaces`/`sort-object-types` rules) as an optional automated-enforcement tool per "Library awareness", not a requirement. `breaking: no`

### 0.3.0 - 2026-07-17

- `2026-07-17 | conventions | patch | no | sanity-check-indirect-phrasing`: the "sanity check requests" bullet added earlier today only covered the literal phrase; a follow-up question surfaced the actual pattern behind it, phrases like "does this make sense," "am I right," "am I missing anything" are grammatically yes/no questions functioning as an invitation to scrutinize the user's own idea, not a literal yes/no about the world (an indirect speech act: surface form and actual intent don't match). Reworded the bullet in root `AGENTS.md` and `template/AGENTS.md` to state that abstraction directly rather than enumerating more trigger phrases, so a shallow "yes" is recognized as failing to answer the actual request. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-17 | repo | minor | no | publish-trigger-and-commit-exception`: the developer has been relocating `## Unreleased changes` entries under a new dated heading by hand, repeatedly, since "release is a manual action" never said who performs the action or on what signal. Defined a publish trigger in `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Versioning, direct phrasing ("publish this", "cut a release") or indirect ("let's have this published", "let's let others propagate this"), the same explicit-plus-indirect pattern already used for session-start signals. On trigger: confirm the rollup bump together, then the agent performs the `CHANGELOG.md` relocation and commits it, a narrow, explicit exception to this repo's otherwise-general "never commit" rule, scoped to this ritual only and available to any agentics contributor, not just this repo's lead developer. Pushing stays with the developer. Commit message: descriptive but succinct, states the version reached; no rigid template, since this repo has no build/compile step, `main` is the artifact, so the commit message doubles as the release notes. `AGENTS.md` § Repo maintenance rules updated to point at this. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-17 | template | minor | no | sanity-check-full-conversation-review`: added "Sanity check requests" to Interaction parameters in root `AGENTS.md` and `template/AGENTS.md`: when asked for a sanity check on an idea, review the whole relevant conversation, not just the latest message, and surface gaps, blind spots, unresolved threads, and edge cases plainly, rather than a shallow restatement or agreement. A specific, named trigger for the existing "push back on bad ideas and identify blind spots" instinct, scoped to a request that explicitly asks for it
- `2026-07-17 | conventions | minor | no | brace-all-control-flow`: added "Always brace control-flow blocks" to `code-style.md` § Conditional logic and functional style, a fifth bullet alongside positive conditions, non-mutational expressions, pure helpers, and data-derived flags: every `if`/`else`/`for`/`while` body gets `{ }` on its own line, even a single statement, since a brace-less one-liner turns a later one-line addition into a diff that rewrites the line's structure instead of being purely additive. Fixed two existing code examples elsewhere in the same file (`getKafkaConfig`, the non-null-assertion example) that predated this rule and used brace-less single-line `if`s, so the file's own examples stay consistent with what it states. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-17 | conventions | minor | no | verify-dated-versioned-facts`: a real session defaulted to OWASP Top 10:2021 despite `security.md`/`security-guidelines.md` already saying "always verify the current edition," the same failure mode already identified once for global dispatch (a passive reminder doesn't reliably fire; see `dispatch-must-be-imperative`), now recurring for a different kind of stale-training-data fact. Generalized past the OWASP case: added "Verifying dated or versioned external facts" to `code-style.md` (after "Dependency version verification", which is the same rule scoped to npm packages): when a fact carries an explicit, checkable version/date marker and verifying it is cheap, check it against the authoritative source at the moment it's about to be cited or applied, don't default to whatever training data implies, and don't rely on a standing reminder to make that happen. Rephrased `security.md`'s OWASP line and `security-guidelines.md`'s header from passive "always verify" into a check triggered at the point of naming or applying a specific edition. `breaking: no`
- `2026-07-17 | conventions | minor | no | terse-conventions-cite-changelog`: convention files were carrying both the rule and the full incident narrative that produced it (which adopter, what broke, exact quotes), duplicating `CHANGELOG.md`, which already records that story once, in more detail, for every entry. Added a design principle to `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Design principles: a convention entry states the rule plus just enough reasoning to judge an edge case it doesn't explicitly cover, and cross-references the originating `CHANGELOG.md` entry by slug instead of re-narrating it. Applied retroactively where safe to separate cleanly: `session-discipline.md` § Verifying conformance, `convention-levels.md` §§ "The reference has to be an instruction, not a citation" and "Local clone or remote URL, same rule either way", `upgrading-adoption.md` § 0's convergent-project example, each trimmed to a `CHANGELOG.md` cross-reference. Left untouched where the embedded reasoning is itself part of the instruction, not narrative color: `convention-levels.md`'s "phrased as an unconditional override" section keeps its tested wording inline since that wording is what must be replicated, not just evidence the rule works. Surveyed `testing.md`, `documentation.md`, `code-review.md`, `security.md`, and `security-guidelines.md`: already reference-oriented with no embedded incident narrative to trim. `breaking: no`: no adopter action required, this only removes duplicated color from files they may have copied, the rules themselves are unchanged
- `2026-07-17 | conventions | minor | no | no-non-null-assertions`: add "No non-null assertions" to `code-style.md`, placed before "TSDoc for exported symbols": never use TypeScript's `variable!` operator, regardless of whether the project's `tsconfig.json` has `strict`/`strictNullChecks` enabled, since the operator silences the compiler without proving the value is non-null rather than actually removing the underlying runtime risk. Prefers optional chaining/nullish coalescing, a narrowing `if`, or a reusable type guard, in that order; existing usages get fixed in scope or logged as tech-debt, same treatment as other new-code-only conventions in this file
- `2026-07-17 | conventions | minor | no | match-existing-config-entry-points`: a real incident in Lyric (an Overture library) surfaced a gap distinct from the existing "library code never reads `process.env`" rule already in `conventions/code-style.md` § Configuration and environment isolation: adding Kafka support read env vars directly inside the new library code, rather than being routed through the host server's existing config-assembly step that already instantiates the library. The boundary rule alone doesn't prevent this, since a genuinely new feature doesn't trigger the general "search for existing patterns" instinct the same way duplicating a utility would; there's nothing to find that looks like "Kafka config" yet. Added "Matching existing configuration entry points" to `code-style.md`, placed directly after "Configuration and environment isolation" and cross-linked with "Searching before writing": before adding config-dependent functionality, locate this project's existing config-resolution mechanism, whatever shape it takes (a single `getXConfig()` helper, or a fuller layered pipeline like Arranger's search-server, constants then env then optional per-catalogue config-file aggregation, each layer overriding the last before the merged result crosses into the library) and extend that, rather than adding a second, parallel entry point. Kept as a separate section rather than folded into either existing one: it's neither the boundary invariant itself nor the generic duplicate-utility search, it's the specific habit of locating a project's established config entry point before assuming a new one is needed
- `2026-07-17 | conventions | minor | no | merge-upstream-check-and-diagnosis`: a live adopter (Arranger) surfaced that the upstream-check mechanism's steady-state path only ever printed `CHANGELOG.md` text for a developer to manually act on, never re-ran the actual structural diagnosis, so a prose-described fix (`CLAUDE.md` collapsing to a stub, a session-file terseness rule) could sit acknowledged in the changelog for a week without ever landing in the project's own files; the no-tag path only "offered" the diagnosis rather than running it, a soft ask with no trace if waved off. Neither path distinguished a pure addition from a real judgment call, so every gap, however trivial, cost the same individual consent, and there was no way to close out a deliberate local customization permanently once reviewed. Fixed: `conventions/convention-levels.md` § Checking for upstream updates now runs `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md` § 1's diagnosis directly, against the current template, whenever there's a non-empty diff since last sync or no tag at all, rather than only surfacing changelog prose or a soft offer; gated by the same existing tiering (mandatory for `agentics_contributor: yes`, opt-in via `propagation_suggestions: yes` elsewhere), no new tier introduced. `upgrading-adoption.md` § 2 replaces the old "no bulk auto-apply, ask about everything individually" rule with two buckets: non-conflicting gaps (a pure addition, or a stale unmodified copy of an older template shape) are batched into one proposed diff behind a single confirmation; genuine conflicts (a deliberate local customization diverging from the template's current handling of the same topic) are still asked about individually, with three choices: adopt upstream, keep local this time, or keep local permanently. A new `.dev/agentics-overrides.md`, created on first permanent decision rather than upfront, records those; § 1 now reads it before reporting a diagnosis so a resolved conflict stops resurfacing. `template/README.md`'s `.dev/` file list documents the new file as created on demand. `breaking: no`: no existing adopter needs to take any action; this changes how the agent's own upgrade-check behaves going forward, not the shape of any file a project has already copied

### 0.2.0 - 2026-07-15

- `2026-07-15 | conventions | patch | no | verify-local-clone-freshness`: § Checking for upstream updates told an agent to "prefer the local Path over the GitHub URL" with nothing verifying the local clone was actually current. A stale local clone reporting "nothing changed" is worse than the remote URL, not better: it hides real updates instead of just being slower to fetch. Fixed to fetch and compare local `HEAD` against the tracked remote branch first, pull if behind, and fall back to reading the remote URL directly if pulling isn't safe, rather than trusting a local copy that was never checked for staleness

- `2026-07-15 | repo | minor | yes | agents-md-single-dispatch-table`: `template/AGENTS.md` was built on the premise that its consumers "cannot fetch a file on demand at all," so it inlined every convention's full content instead of dispatching. That premise was checked, not assumed, and found wrong: the AGENTS.md standard's own guidance recommends the opposite (a "progressive disclosure" pattern, keep the main file lean, reference other documents for on-demand reading), since real AGENTS.md consumers (Cursor, Copilot, Aider) have file-system access like any other coding agent. Rebuilt `template/AGENTS.md` around a single canonical dispatch table. First pass only moved the "When to read what" table itself; a follow-up look at the same files found the _rest_ of `CLAUDE.md` (Interaction parameters, Critical constraints, Memory hygiene, Initialization) was still a verbatim second copy of content `AGENTS.md` also carried, the same drift risk in miniature. Collapsed `template/CLAUDE.md` to a minimal stub: it exists only because Claude Code auto-loads it, and holds nothing but a pointer to `AGENTS.md` now, which is the sole canonical source for all of it, project-specific content included. Applied the identical collapse to the root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` pair, and fixed both files' own self-description lines, which had been telling Claude to "prefer CLAUDE.md," backwards once CLAUDE.md became a stub pointing back to AGENTS.md. Corrected every place that still described the old model: `README.md` (§ Two tiers, § What gets installed, "should contain only project-specific content"), `template/README.md` (file table, adoption order — `AGENTS.md` is now the required copy, `CLAUDE.md` optional, reversing the prior guidance — the "already have an existing setup" and "contributing back" sections), `CONTRIBUTING.md` (§ Design principles' "Lean project files" and the drift-risk bullet, the role-file creation steps), `conventions/convention-levels.md`, `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md`. `breaking: yes`: adopters who copied the old inlined `AGENTS.md`, or who have project-specific content sitting in `CLAUDE.md`, need to move it to `AGENTS.md` and reduce `CLAUDE.md` to the stub

- `2026-07-15 | repo | minor | no | changelog-single-version-source`: added a `bump: patch | minor` field to the CHANGELOG entry format (this file's own header, above), with a `major` tier reserved until productization is explicitly authorized. Classified by whether an entry fixes/clarifies/backfills something already intended (`patch`) or introduces a rule/file/procedure/section that didn't exist before (`minor`) — a change that feels major-sized gets flagged to the developer explicitly rather than silently downgraded. `CHANGELOG.md` is the only place a version number exists at all: `## Released` nests dated versions as `###` sub-headings, with `## Unreleased changes` as its own top-level section. Nothing else stamps a copy of the number: root `AGENTS.md`'s tag and `template/AGENTS.md`'s tag are both pointers (`<!-- agentics-version: see CHANGELOG.md § Released (latest entry) -->` and the template equivalent), and `CLAUDE.md` (root and template) and `template/CLAUDE.softeng.md` carry no tag at all, since none of them are the canonical source. `template/AGENTS.md`'s pointer only resolves while the file sits inside this repo; adoption is the point where it gets replaced with an actual stamped snapshot (`X.Y.Z | synced: <sha>`), documented in `template/README.md` § Keeping up to date, since a copied file can't dynamically reference this repo's `CHANGELOG.md` from somewhere else. Added the full mechanism to `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Versioning; fixed the same section's own root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` "Repo maintenance rules" bullet, which had named only `template/CLAUDE.md`'s tag despite `template/AGENTS.md` carrying an independent one

- `2026-07-15 | conventions | patch | no | migration-is-a-cutover`: a live adopter's agent, running `upgrading-adoption.md`'s no-synced-value migration path, split `.dev/sessions.md` into per-day files correctly (right historical timestamps, a real timestamp on the new file for today) but left the old `sessions.md` in place alongside the new `.dev/sessions/` directory, plus a stray `.gitkeep` inside it. Root cause: the migration bullet said "split by date" but never said the old file should be removed once every entry had a new home, and never mentioned `.gitkeep` at all — the agent did exactly what it was told and stopped where the instructions stopped. Added to `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md` § 2: this is a cutover, not an addition; delete `.dev/sessions.md` once migrated, and don't leave a placeholder file behind once the directory holds real content. `breaking: no`: adopters who already completed this migration correctly (deleted the source themselves) are unaffected; this only closes the gap for future migrations

- `2026-07-15 | conventions | patch | no | property-ordering-insertion-scope`: a live adopter placed a new `network:` top-level key at its YAML edit point rather than its alphabetical position in `values.yaml` — not a misunderstanding of the alphabetize rule itself, but of where to insert when adding a key to an existing file. The existing `Apply when writing new config content...` paragraph's only insertion-position instruction used "resource block", which is Terraform terminology, leaving the equivalent case for YAML keys unstated. Generalised the sentence in `conventions/code-style.md` to name both: "new key or resource block", "not at the current edit point, not at the end of the file". `breaking: no`: adopters who copied the prior wording got the spirit (alphabetize), just not the explicit note about insertion position for YAML keys

- `2026-07-14 | template | minor | no | development-md-gh-cli-prereq`: onboarding a new contributor surfaced a gap: `template/DEVELOPMENT.md`'s Prerequisites and Setup sections were entirely blank TODOs, with nothing filled in even for a near-universal need. Added GitHub CLI (`gh`), authenticated, as a default Prerequisites bullet and a one-line Setup step (`gh auth login`), since any agent working in an agentics-adopting repo relies on `gh` for PR/issue work on the developer's behalf, and a missing auth session is the kind of thing that stalls a first request rather than announcing itself upfront. Kept as documentation, not a new onboarding-wizard question: it's a one-time local machine check a human reads, not a preference the agent needs to record in project memory the way role/softeng/propagation are

- `2026-07-14 | conventions | minor | yes | design-review-and-derived-flags`: prompted by feedback from a different adopting project (helm-charts): asked "why didn't you offer this better approach unprompted," where the better approach was replacing a redundant `type: gateway | ingress | none` discriminator with behaviour derived from whether `gateway.parentRef.name` or `ingress.hosts` was actually set. Two separate things, not one. First, a genuinely reusable design principle, generalized past Helm: added "Derive from data, don't duplicate as a flag" to `conventions/code-style.md` § Conditional logic and functional style as a fourth bullet, applying to Helm values, API schemas, and configuration objects generally — when presence unambiguously implies intent, don't require intent to be stated twice. Second, and closer to the actual complaint: "Surface better alternatives as options; let the user decide" already existed in Interaction parameters but named no trigger, so it only fired reactively, after being asked why an alternative wasn't offered, rather than before presenting an implementation. Reworded in `template/CLAUDE.md` and `template/AGENTS.md` to name the timing explicitly. While fixing `AGENTS.md`, found it was also missing the entire "Verify purpose alignment before implementing" bullet that `CLAUDE.md` has, a real propagation gap independent of this incident; added it. `breaking: yes`: adopters who copied `AGENTS.md`'s Interaction parameters before this fix are missing a bullet other adopters (via `CLAUDE.md` dispatch) already had

- `2026-07-14 | conventions | minor | yes | session-entry-format-agents-parity`: a live adopter's agent (Arranger) logged a session file naming an individual by GitHub handle twice, listing five dead commit SHAs from someone else's already-squash-merged branch, and narrating a PR review rather than the one real local change (a tech-debt entry) it produced. Two independent gaps. First, `template/AGENTS.md` was missing the entire § Session file entry format section: "write about effects, not style," "name code, not people," the bullet format, all of it, jumping straight from timestamp rules to tech-debt format — any agent relying on `AGENTS.md` alone (no dispatch to `CLAUDE.md`) never saw these rules at all. Backfilled in condensed form matching the file's existing style. Second, `conventions/session-discipline.md`'s "do not log conversational activity" only ever named the _no local change_ case, leaving the mixed case (one real change alongside a lot of no-op investigation) unaddressed; added "Mixed reviews: log the local effect, not the investigation" to § Session file entry format. `breaking: yes`: adopters who copied `AGENTS.md` before this fix are missing session-log formatting rules other adopters (via `CLAUDE.md` dispatch) already had

- `2026-07-14 | conventions | minor | no | immutability-critical-constraint-exception`: the same incident's file was rewritten in place after its day had closed, to remove the individual's name and dead external references, breaking the letter of "a session file is immutable once its day is done." That was the right call, not a violation of it: an individual's name is a Critical Constraint violation (same tier as a credential), not an ordinary quality problem, and immutability was designed to stop a later session quietly rewriting an earlier one's judgment calls, not to protect a Critical Constraint violation from correction. Added "Exception: a Critical Constraint violation overrides immutability" to `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/AGENTS.md`, naming the credential-in-an-old-commit analogy directly so the boundary isn't left to case-by-case judgment next time

- `2026-07-13 | conventions | patch | no | session-timestamp-no-time-source`: found a real, systemic instance in an adopting project (all of its `.dev/sessions/` files, not just today's, carried `T000000`): the convention named the `YYYY-MM-DDTHHMMSS.md` filename format but never told the agent how to obtain the actual time. An agent can be handed today's date via context but has no innate sense of wall-clock time, so the unaddressed `HHMMSS` portion got padded with zeros rather than fetched. Fixed in `conventions/session-discipline.md` § Session file identity and the matching `AGENTS.md` section: added an explicit instruction to run a shell command (e.g. `date +%Y-%m-%dT%H%M%S`) before creating a new session file, and named the all-zero-timestamp pattern as the tell that this step is being skipped, not a coincidence. Also added an explicit exception for migrating or backfilling historical entries (splitting a legacy shared log into per-day files, reconstructing past work): the real time genuinely isn't recoverable there, so `T000000` is a legitimate placeholder for that case, not something to retroactively rename once the fix is in place for new files. Reordered both sections so "check whether a file for today already exists" comes before "get the real time": the original ordering placed the time-fetch instruction ahead of the existence check it depends on, which could read as "always fetch a time and create a file" instead of "only fetch and create when the check says none exists yet". A live adopter then surfaced a further gap the same day: told about this fix, its agent "corrected" its own already-existing today's-file by renaming it to the current time as a "best approximation of session start" — the same guessing anti-pattern in a new disguise, since the file's real creation time was already unrecoverable and relabelling it with a fabricated later time doesn't fix that. Added "Never rename an already-created file, today's own included" to both files, naming this exact case

- `2026-07-10 | repo | patch | no | readme-ai-native-plus-collaboration`: added two sentences to `README.md`'s opening, positioning agentics against the "AI-native documentation" practice (structuring content so an agent can discover, parse, and act on it reliably) while naming the thing that practice alone doesn't cover: conventions that shape how the agent reasons and collaborates, not just what it can parse (questioning ideas, surfacing alternatives, checking purpose alignment before committing). Surfaced while drafting an unrelated performance objective and realizing the same framing gap existed in the pending README wording before it was ever applied

- `2026-07-10 | repo | patch | no | adopt-agentics-not-pieces`: a positioning nuance, not just word choice: people adopt agentics (fully or partially), not "conventions from agentics" — the latter frames it as a menu to pick items from rather than a system to bring in. Found one real instance in `convention-levels.md` § How much to keep locally, fixed. Added "Adopt agentics, not pieces of it" to `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Design principles so future writing doesn't drift back into the piecemeal framing

- `2026-07-10 | conventions | patch | no | validate-remote-only-fetch`: tested the imperative-fetch pattern against a fixture with zero local agentics clone available, session-start checklist pointed only at a raw GitHub URL. Passed first attempt: fetched the real current content, derived the correct checklist from it, correctly flagged that the propagation/contributor-status step couldn't be evaluated (no global context existed for the simulated user) rather than silently skipping it, and independently noticed the `justincorrigible` vs `oicr-softeng` URL discrepancy without being prompted to look for it. Noted as tested in `conventions/convention-levels.md` § How much to keep locally

- `2026-07-10 | conventions | minor | no | two-tier-locality-model`: documented an efficiency model that had been implicit but never stated: what decides whether an adopting project needs a local copy of a convention versus a live pointer to agentics is how often it's needed (every session, no natural trigger vs. only when actually doing that task), not how much content it has. Session-discipline and the project's own version/sync marker are the only things that must be locally reliable; testing, code style, security, documentation, code review, convention-levels, and upgrading-adoption all stay live pointers regardless of whether agentics is a local clone or a remote URL. `AGENTS.md`'s full inlining is the one deliberate exception, for agents that cannot fetch on demand at all. Written up in `README.md` § Two tiers (motivation) and `conventions/convention-levels.md` § How much to keep locally (operational rule)

- `2026-07-10 | conventions | patch | yes | tech-debt-fix-field`: yesterday's "separate issue from fix" rule was added as prose without updating the format it governs: the minimal tech-debt skeleton still only had `[description] / standalone / context`, no slot for the fix itself. A real incident during a new project's initialization (overture/docs) produced an entry whose `context:` field pointed at tracked work with no statement of the fix at all, which the skeleton's own field spec (`roadmap item reference or brief note`) reads as sufficient. Added an explicit `fix:` line to the skeleton in `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/AGENTS.md`. Redirecting to where the full fix is tracked (a roadmap item, a design doc) is still valid and often correct; what's required is that the field itself also states what the fix is, even in one sentence, rather than a bare pointer standing in for it with nothing else. Breaking: existing entries written against the old skeleton are missing the field, though nothing requires backfilling them
- `2026-07-10 | conventions | minor | no | verify-conformance-not-structure`: same incident's self-diagnosis (the agent that wrote the non-conforming entry, asked directly why) traced the cause further: reading a convention and holding it as an active constraint while generating content several steps later in the same turn are not the same act, and nothing forced the second one to happen, especially in a multi-artifact batch like initialization. Added "Verifying conformance, not just structure" to `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/AGENTS.md`: re-read each governed artifact against the convention's specific prose requirement before finalizing it, as a discrete step, not each time the rule was merely read earlier

- `2026-07-10 | repo | minor | no | new-convention-file-checklist`: the orphaned-references fix (previous entry) was three files falling through the same gap: adding a new `conventions/*.md` file has no enforced checklist ensuring it's actually wired into `CLAUDE.md`'s dispatch table, `AGENTS.md`'s inline coverage, and `template/README.md`'s file manifest. Added as step 5 of `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Proposing changes, naming the three real instances (security.md, documentation.md, upgrading-adoption.md) this already happened to

- `2026-07-10 | template | patch | yes | fix-orphaned-references`: audited `CLAUDE.md`'s dispatch table against `AGENTS.md`'s actual coverage and against the `conventions/` directory listing, plus `template/README.md`'s file manifest against both. Found and fixed: `AGENTS.md` had no "Documentation" or "Deploying or debugging a service" section despite both being dispatched from `CLAUDE.md`; `conventions/security.md` (credentials policy, Node.js/pnpm supply-chain hardening, quick threat model) was never referenced from `CLAUDE.md`'s dispatch table or `AGENTS.md`'s Security section at all, only `security-guidelines.md` was, making the pnpm guidance genuinely unreachable since it was added; `template/README.md`'s file table omitted `code-review.md`, `documentation.md`, and `upgrading-adoption.md` entirely. Fixed all in `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `template/README.md`. Breaking: any adopter who copied the dispatch table before this fix is missing the security.md line and the two new AGENTS.md sections

- `2026-07-10 | conventions | minor | no | writing-for-a-cold-reader`: added a "Writing for a cold reader" section to `conventions/documentation.md` (retitled from "Documentation structure" to "Documentation" since it now covers more than structure): documentation written during or right after a live design discussion inherits that discussion's dense, backward-referencing register, which works for whoever was in the room and against anyone reading cold. Root-caused via real feedback from a dev and a non-dev reader to register, not information density. Five rules (one idea per sentence, conclusion first, restate rather than reference, worked examples in their own block, minimize cross-reference chains), scoped explicitly to design docs/tech-debt/roadmap and explicitly excluding `.dev/sessions/` logs, which are correctly terse for an already-oriented reader

- `2026-07-10 | conventions | minor | no | git-staging-transparency`: real confusion while working with an adopted project: the developer relied on git staging to review changes incrementally, expected changes to be staged automatically, and instead found nothing staged while being told "the changes are there" — because agents don't stage without being asked, same as they don't commit, but that wasn't stated anywhere. Filled in `code-style.md § Git`, previously an empty team placeholder, with real default guidance: never stage without explicit request, and always state a change's actual git state (staged vs. unstaged) plainly rather than ambiguously confirming it exists

- `2026-07-10 | conventions | patch | no | dashes-structural-exceptions`: propagated a refinement from the developer's personal global convention: `code-style.md § Dashes` explicitly named `-` bullet markers, `---` horizontal dividers, markdown table separator rows, compound-word hyphens, and numeric ranges as acceptable structural exceptions, rather than leaving space-hyphen-space vs. bullet-prefix as a "case-by-case review." Also added a heading/title example (colon, not dash separator) that wasn't covered before, and fixed a stale `sessions.md` reference in the bullet-annotation example to `.dev/sessions/`

- `2026-07-09 | template | patch | no | fix-canadian-yze-spelling`: `template/CLAUDE.softeng.md`, `template/AGENTS.md`, and `template/conventions/code-style.md` all stated Canadian English uses `-yse` (analyse, paralyse), framed as a divergence from American `-yze` parallel to the real `-ise`/`-ize` split. A cited authoritative source (Toronto Metropolitan University's Author's Guide) confirms the opposite: Canadian usage follows `-yze` (analyze, paralyze, catalyze), matching American English, with no divergence for this suffix family. Corrected all three; also fixed accordingly in the developer's own `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (outside this repo, not itself a template change)
- `2026-07-09 | conventions | patch | no | recommend-fresh-context-after-migration`: `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md` § 3 now recommends starting a fresh chat/session for subsequent work after the procedure completes, if the harness gives no way to reset context: a full diagnosis-and-migration run reads enough file content and diffs to add lasting token weight with no ongoing value once applied. Extends the existing "new thread is cheaper than accumulating" reasoning in `session-discipline.md` rather than introducing a new principle
- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | no | tech-debt-issue-fix-separation`: propagated from a real project's tech-debt.md (51 entries using a richer File/Severity/Kind/Issue/Fix/Standalone structure, 3 older entries still in the minimal form): separating issue from fix is worth doing at any scale and costs nothing extra; the fuller structure is a genuine tradeoff that pays off with dozens of entries and costs more friction than it's worth with a handful. Documented both in `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/AGENTS.md` § Tech-debt entry format as a scale-dependent choice, not a default upgrade

- `2026-07-09 | repo | minor | no | explicit-agent-audience`: none of the four primary agent-facing entry points (`CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`, root and template) explicitly told a human reader they weren't the intended audience — they talked about or to agents, but never explicitly ruled a person out, relying on filename convention alone. Added an explicit "for AI agents" line to each, pointing a human reader to `README.md`/`CONTRIBUTING.md` instead. Marked `docs/agent-security.md` as intentionally dual-audience rather than leaving it ambiguous. Added "Explicit audience" as a design principle in `CONTRIBUTING.md` for future agent-facing files

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | patch | no | separate-detection-from-tier`: a real Arranger session's own explanation of why the check ran conflated "this project mentions agentics" (adoption detection, a property of the project) with "the check is mandatory for me" (contributor status, a property of the agent's global context) — it still reached the right answer, but the phrasing showed the two ideas sit close enough in `convention-levels.md` § Checking for upstream updates to blur together. Reordered the section so detection is answered first and stated as a project property, then tier strength second and stated as an agent property, with an explicit line saying a project mentioning agentics does not by itself make the check mandatory

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | no | verify-before-writing-dev-docs`: propagated from a real project session (Arranger): verifying against actual code/file state before marking a roadmap or tech-debt item done/resolved held up well across a full session, not just once. Added to `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/AGENTS.md` § Keeping `.dev/` current: before writing a status change, verify against current reality, not a prior description or session summary

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | patch | no | contributor-check-explicit-override`: the mandatory contributor tier (previous entry) still lost to a project's own complete session-start checklist, the same pattern that blocked the opt-in tier before. Confirmed by rebuilding that exact blocking pattern in a throwaway fixture: a global instruction phrased as "run this every session" was ignored in favour of the project's own explicit checklist; rephrased to state the override directly — name the failure mode (complete local checklist, narrower dispatch line naming other topics) and say neither opts a project out — and it worked. Added this requirement to `conventions/convention-levels.md` § Checking for upstream updates as an explicit warning with the tested wording pattern, since a plain "mandatory" flag reads as a frequency instruction, not a precedence one

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | yes | contributor-mandatory-upstream-check`: split "checking for upstream updates" into two tiers: opt-in (`propagation_suggestions: yes`, existing behaviour, unchanged) and mandatory for agentics contributors (`agentics_contributor: yes`) — for contributors the check now runs every session in every project regardless of that project's own opt-in setting, and keeps recurring for an unresolved gap instead of surfacing once and going quiet. Stops only on an explicit `agentics_upstream_check: no`, recorded per-project ("stop for this project") or in global context ("stop for all projects") — never inferred from silence. `agentics_contributor: yes` itself now needs recording in global context rather than only agentics' own project memory, since it must apply across every project a contributor works in, not just sessions inside agentics. Updated `conventions/convention-levels.md`, `conventions/session-discipline.md`, `template/AGENTS.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and both root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` initialization blocks. Breaking: contributors who already set `agentics_contributor: yes` only in agentics' project memory need to re-record it in their global context for the mandatory tier to actually apply elsewhere

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | patch | no | restore-unreleased-catchup`: blind-testing the no-tag path against a clean fixture (no legacy checklist blocking it) surfaced that the "surface current `## Unreleased` as a bounded first-encounter catch-up" behavior had been lost when `convention-levels.md` was refactored to defer to `upgrading-adoption.md`: each file pointed at the other for "the surfacing/catch-up treatment," and neither actually contained it anymore. Moved the concrete instruction into `upgrading-adoption.md` § 1's "Version tag" bullet (the one place that's actually reached on this path); `convention-levels.md` now points at that specific location instead of a vague "see the other file"

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | no | upgrade-diagnosis-cross-file-drift`: running `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md` for real on a pre-agentics project surfaced two gaps the procedure didn't cover: `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` had independently drifted to different step counts for the same checklist (each looked fine compared to the template in isolation, not compared to each other), and a third agent-specific file (`.github/copilot-instructions.md`) that the project's own contributor docs say to keep in sync was invisible to the procedure entirely. Added both as explicit diagnosis items in § 1

- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | no | upgrade-diagnosis-legacy-checklist`: live-testing on a real pre-agentics project (its `CLAUDE.md` has a complete "Starting a session" checklist and an explicit "Workflow" dispatch line that names specific topics deferred to global, not including session discipline) showed the automatic upstream-update check correctly does not fire there — a project's own explicit, narrower instructions take priority over global dispatch, as they should. Added this exact pattern to `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md` § 1 as a named diagnostic signal, so it's recognized as "needs migration" rather than treated as an unexplained gap
- `2026-07-09 | repo | minor | no | no-personal-identifying-details`: found a personal GitHub username and a contributor's first name leaked into committed docs (`CHANGELOG.md`, two `.dev/sessions/` files) during this session's own work; scrubbed to generic placeholders. Root cause: root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` (governing this repo's own content) never had the "no machine-specific paths" constraint that `template/CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` already had for downstream adopters — added it there too, broadened in both places to explicitly cover usernames and individuals' real names, not just paths. Added a matching design principle and pre-commit grep step to `CONTRIBUTING.md`
- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | no | upgrading-adoption`: add `conventions/upgrading-adoption.md`: a defined, on-demand procedure for bringing a project's agentics integration current, replacing one-off hand-written migration prompts. Covers confirming adoption (including projects that share the lineage with zero textual link to agentics, e.g. Usher — predates any tag and independently reimplements the same session-start checklist), diagnosing tag/session-file/`AGENTS.md`-completeness/agent-neutrality gaps against the current template, and applying fixes with per-change consent. `conventions/convention-levels.md` § Checking for upstream updates now defers to it for the "tag missing or stale" case instead of describing the fix inline; wired into `template/CLAUDE.md`'s dispatch table and a new `template/AGENTS.md` section
- `2026-07-09 | conventions | minor | no | dispatch-must-be-imperative`: found, by live-testing the global-dispatch pattern added yesterday, that a personal global context citing agentics as "the base convention" does not reliably cause an agent to read agentics' current content: a real session in an adopting project skipped every agentics-specific session-start step with no error, because the citation reads as documentation rather than an instruction. Added guidance to `conventions/convention-levels.md` § Global: phrase the dispatch as an action ("at every session-start signal, read `<path>` fresh and follow it"), with a direct path where possible rather than routing through another lookup. Fixed accordingly in the developer's own `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (outside this repo, not itself a template change)
- `2026-07-08 | conventions | minor | yes | sessions-per-contributor-file`: replace the single shared `.dev/sessions.md` with `.dev/sessions/`, one file per contributor per day (`YYYY-MM-DDTHHMMSS.md`, no descriptive slug): filename uniqueness is what prevents cross-contributor merge conflicts, not smaller files or a slug. Session-start checklist now reads the most recent 1-2 files by sorted filename instead of a shared entry list; authorship for "is this file mine" is determined via `git log`/`git config user.email`, never written into file content. Updates `template/conventions/session-discipline.md`, `template/AGENTS.md`, `template/README.md`, `template/DEVELOPMENT.md`, `template/CLAUDE.roles/*.md`, `template/conventions/{convention-levels,security,security-guidelines}.md`, `template/global-context/{roadmap,security-guidelines}.md`, and root `README.md`/`AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`. Downstream adopters need to migrate their own `.dev/sessions.md` (see this repo's own `.dev/sessions/` for a worked example of a retroactive split).
- `2026-07-08 | conventions | patch | no | agent-neutral-drift-fix`: found three template files violating the already-documented "Agent-neutral" principle (CONTRIBUTING.md): `conventions/convention-levels.md` and `global-context/projects.md` hardcoded `~/.claude/...` with no Claude/other-agent parenthetical, and `conventions/security.md` additionally still referenced the pre-rename `personal-context/` path (renamed to `global-context/` on 2026-06-05). Fixed all three to the dual-framing pattern already used correctly elsewhere (e.g. `global-context/README.md`). Added a `grep -rn '~/\.claude\|Claude Code' template/` pre-commit check to `CONTRIBUTING.md` § Proposing changes, since the principle being written down didn't stop it drifting back
- `2026-07-08 | conventions | minor | yes | upstream-update-check`: implement the Phase 2 "update propagation" roadmap item (design was previously open): adopting projects extend their `agentics-template-version` tag with `| synced: <commit-sha>`; with `propagation_suggestions: yes`, session start diffs `CHANGELOG.md` since that commit via the local agentics clone (no full-file read unless something changed), surfaces new entries with `breaking: yes` ones called out first, and advances the marker once reviewed. Adoption is detected via the tag OR just a mention of agentics in `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` (e.g. the "Adapted from softeng/agentics" line), so a project with no tag at all still gets caught rather than silently skipped. Existing adopters with no `synced` value (or no tag) get the current `## Unreleased` section surfaced as a one-time bounded catch-up (not a full historical diff, but not silently swallowed either), plus an offer to add/repair the tag, then baseline to `HEAD`. Adds `conventions/convention-levels.md` § Checking for upstream updates; wires a checklist step into `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/AGENTS.md`; rewrites "Keeping up to date" in both READMEs. Breaking because the tag format itself changed shape (`X.Y.Z` → `X.Y.Z | synced: <sha>`)
- `2026-07-08 | conventions | minor | no | repo-url-local-first`: add `Repo URL` field and a local-first resolution rule to `global-context/projects.md`: prefer a project's local clone over fetching its GitHub URL, since local may be ahead of origin; verify freshness with `git log @{u}..` first unless the repo is sole-maintained
- `2026-07-08 | conventions | patch | no | name-code-not-people-backfill`: the `2026-07-07 | name-code-not-people` entry below claimed the rule was added to `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/CLAUDE.md`; neither file actually had it. Added it to both now. Left the original entry as-is (immutable historical record) rather than editing it after the fact.
- `2026-07-08 | conventions | minor | no | global-dispatch-not-duplicate`: add guidance to `conventions/convention-levels.md`: a global agent context that has adopted agentics as its base should reference the template for conventions it already covers rather than holding a full duplicate copy, keeping only genuinely local refinements or stated overrides. A duplicated convention drifts silently when one copy is updated and the other isn't.

- `2026-07-08 | conventions | patch | no | dash-rule-absolute`: strengthen dash avoidance in code-style.md and session-discipline.md: rule is now absolute with no conversational-chat exemption; drafts and chat responses are explicitly included since they are routinely pasted or submitted; drops "persisted prose" scope qualifier

- `2026-07-08 | template | patch | no | general-role-label`: clarify "general" role option in README.md and template/CLAUDE.md initialization block: add "(non-code work)" parenthetical so new adopters understand what the option covers without reading the role file first

- `2026-07-08 | conventions | minor | no | code-review`: add `conventions/code-review.md`: pre-review gate requiring purpose, layer ownership, and necessity to be established before examining implementation; update `template/CLAUDE.md` dispatch to split "writing code or doing reviews" into separate writing and reviewing entries (reviewing reads both code-style and code-review); add inline "Code review" section to `template/AGENTS.md`

- `2026-07-07 | conventions | minor | no | global-roadmap-split`: add `global-context/roadmap.md` template and convention for splitting global roadmaps by work context (e.g. `roadmap-work.md` vs `roadmap-personal.md`); extend session-start checklist in `session-discipline.md` step 2 to read only the roadmap relevant to the current project context; update `global-context/README.md` file list

- `2026-07-07 | conventions | minor | no | documentation-structure`: add `conventions/documentation.md` defining the two-tier docs model (`/docs` for consumers, `.dev/docs` for contributors), cross-linking over duplication, and the rule that `/docs` links to `.dev/docs` files must use full GitHub URLs (relative paths break on hosted docs sites); add dispatch entry to `template/CLAUDE.md`

- `2026-07-07 | conventions | minor | no | name-code-not-people`: add "Name code, not people" rule to `conventions/session-discipline.md` and `template/CLAUDE.md`: attribute work to features, modules, and systems - not to individuals; attribution belongs in git history, not in sessions.md, tech-debt, docs, or any other persisted content

- `2026-07-07 | template | minor | no | no-machine-specific-paths`: add "No machine- or user-specific absolute paths in committed files" to Critical constraints in template/CLAUDE.md and template/AGENTS.md: a resolved local resource path (e.g. a per-project memory path) breaks for other developers, other machines, and after the repo moves; use a generic placeholder instead. Found via a leaked Claude project-memory path committed in a downstream project's CLAUDE.md.

- `2026-06-30 | conventions | minor | no | dependency-version-verification`: add "Dependency version verification" to template/conventions/code-style.md after Library awareness: always run `npm view <package> version` (or equivalent) before writing a version into any config; do not use versions from training data; "already used in this repo" does not imply currency

- `2026-06-30 | template | minor | no | purpose-alignment`: add "verify purpose alignment before implementing" to Interaction parameters in template/CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.md, and AGENTS.md: when a task names a goal, the agent must check whether the chosen approach achieves that goal directly rather than something adjacent; lead with the gap as an objection before writing anything

- `2026-06-30 | repo | minor | no | agent-security-docs`: add `docs/agent-security.md` covering 7 AI agent attack vectors (configuration injection/CBSE, indirect prompt injection, Comment and Control, MCP server poisoning, supply chain attacks, self-replicating worms, memory/RAG poisoning); session-start integrity check guidance; what agents cannot catch automatically; threat intelligence sources; extend session-discipline.md git log check to include `.claude/settings.json`

- `2026-06-28 | conventions | minor | no | session-effects-not-style`: add "write about effects, not style" rule to conventions/session-discipline.md: session bullets must describe what the code now does for operators, users, or callers; style choices, refactoring approach, and helper names are noise

- `2026-06-28 | conventions | minor | no | functional-style`: add "Conditional logic and functional style" to conventions/code-style.md: guidance to write code where the happy path is immediately visible, values come from expressions not mutation, and reusable logic is isolated in named functions; includes TypeScript before/after example

- `2026-06-28 | softeng | minor | no | np-client-ingress`: add NetworkPolicy client ingress check to CLAUDE.softeng.md stateful-service deployment steps: operators generate NPs for their own internal traffic, not for application clients; always add a supplemental kustomize NP allowing client pods to reach the service port; common ports listed; section numbered 3 (deployment-order renumbered to 4)

- `2026-06-27 | conventions | minor | no | pnpm-supply-chain`: add Node.js/pnpm supply chain section to conventions/security.md: pnpm v10+ blocks install scripts by default (A08 control); always set confirm-modules-purge=false and scarf-js-opt-out=true in .npmrc; use allowBuilds in pnpm-workspace.yaml to explicitly approve packages that need install scripts; @scarf/scarf always false

- `2026-06-26 | conventions | minor | no | tsdoc-for-exports`: add TSDoc for exported symbols convention to conventions/code-style.md: all exported functions, types, and interfaces require a brief TSDoc comment; distinct from the no-comments rule (which applies to inline implementation code); inline member comments for non-obvious fields; apply to new exports, log as tech-debt if missing on existing exports

- `2026-06-26 | template | patch | no | optional-bootstrapping-files`: clarify that conventions/, CLAUDE.roles/, and CLAUDE.softeng.md are bootstrapping artifacts, not permanent per-project files: skip them if your agent's global context already defines universal conventions, role, and team conventions; project CLAUDE.md should contain only project-specific content; updated template/CLAUDE.md dispatch table, template/README.md How-to-adopt section, and root README.md file manifest

- `2026-06-26 | conventions | minor | no | fix-small-things-immediately`: add "fix in place vs. log as tech-debt" distinction to scope discipline: if a scope-adjacent issue can be fixed in a minute without risk, fix it; tech-debt is for genuinely deferred work, not one-liners; updated conventions/code-style.md and AGENTS.md

- `2026-06-26 | repo | patch | no | global-settings-note`: add note to README explaining that teams with a global ~/.claude/settings.json should move the PreToolUse credential hook there instead of copying the per-project .claude/settings.json into each repo

- `2026-06-25 | template | minor | no | dev-docs-structure`: add `.dev/docs/` pattern to DEVELOPMENT.md and CLAUDE.md: per-service subdirectory (e.g. `docs/postgres/`), root `index.md`, read the relevant guide before deploying or debugging a specific service

- `2026-06-25 | template | patch | no | direct-opposition`: strengthen pushback convention in CLAUDE.md interaction parameters: lead with the objection rather than a neutral trade-off list; add explicit "no ego" framing; direct "this is not a good idea because X" is more useful than diplomatic hedging

- `2026-06-25 | softeng | minor | no | naming-conventions`: fill in "Naming conventions" section of CLAUDE.softeng.md: psql-db/mongo-db/vso companion naming pattern; Helm release names match helm/ subdirectory; TF resource names match release names
- `2026-06-25 | softeng | minor | no | tooling-ci-registry`: fill in remaining tooling TODO in CLAUDE.softeng.md: CI/Jenkins/tfDeploy.groovy, ghcr.io registry, softeng OCI chart repo, stateful operator/chart table (CNPG, MongoDB Community, opensearch-k8s-operator, Strimzi, Keycloak/keycloakx)

- `2026-06-23 | softeng | minor | no | stateful-deploy-guide`: add "Deploying stateful services" section to CLAUDE.softeng.md: Jenkins CRD access (aggregation vs manual ClusterRole entry; config-jenkins-instances pattern), credential bootstrap direction (CNPG auto-generates and you copy to Vault; MongoDB Community requires Vault pre-seeded before operator applies), deployment order rule
- `2026-06-23 | conventions | patch | no | tf-resource-ordering`: extend property-ordering rule to Terraform resource blocks: alphabetize by resource name; VSO companion follows its primary; insertion point anchor must not override alphabetical placement

- `2026-06-22 | conventions | minor | no | sessions-immutability`: add sessions.md append-only rule to session-discipline.md: only today's entry is editable; prior entries are immutable historical record; revise before the session ends, not later
- `2026-06-22 | conventions | minor | no | dev-docs-staleness-pass`: add staleness pass to session-start checklist in session-discipline.md: mark completed roadmap items done, close resolved PINNEDs, remove addressed tech-debt before starting new work
- `2026-06-22 | softeng | minor | no | keycloak-exception`: add Keycloak exception to operator-first rule: use codecentric/keycloakx chart; Keycloak Operator is namespace-scoped by default and incompatible with shared cluster-wide DevOps installation model
- `2026-06-22 | softeng | minor | no | operator-preferences`: replace no-Bitnami wording with positive preference (project-owned and community operators over vendor-bundled charts); remove Keycloak Operator from operator-first list; add PINNED roadmap entry format
- `2026-06-22 | softeng | minor | no | tf-root-structure`: add Terraform root structure convention to CLAUDE.softeng.md: exactly two roots per environment (stateless/stateful); management-plane TF providers (e.g. mrparkers/keycloak) go in stateless alongside hashicorp/helm, not in a third root
- `2026-06-21 | conventions | minor | no | sessions-entry-format`: add sessions.md entry format to session-discipline.md: one context sentence + one bullet per file/logical group; decisions and constraints inline only when non-obvious; no prose paragraphs
- `2026-06-21 | softeng | minor | no | k8s-infra-conventions`: add Kubernetes infrastructure conventions to CLAUDE.softeng.md tooling preferences: operator-first for stateful services (CNPG, MongoDB Community Operator, opensearch-k8s-operator, Strimzi; PINNED items over standalone workarounds); VSO-everywhere for secret injection (direct Vault access and TF data sources are anti-patterns; -vso companion releases mandatory)
- `2026-06-19 | conventions | minor | no | structured-logging`: add structured logging convention to AGENTS.md and conventions/code-style.md: emit key-value pairs or JSON, mandatory fields, mandatory for auth/access/export/error boundaries, set up before application logic
- `2026-06-19 | conventions | minor | no | property-ordering`: add property ordering convention to AGENTS.md and conventions/code-style.md: alphabetize config properties at all nesting levels
- `2026-06-19 | conventions | patch | no | language-spelling`: expand Language entry in AGENTS.md to mention spelling convention placeholder with Canadian English example
- `2026-06-19 | template | minor | no | lean-project-agents`: establish lean project AGENTS.md pattern: project-specific content inline, universal conventions compressed to pointers to agentics template; demonstrated in Arranger AGENTS.md
- `2026-06-19 | template | minor | no | role-profiles`: add CLAUDE.roles/ with dev.md, general.md, bio.md, ai-eng.md; role question added to initialization block in CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md; general.md serves as foundation for non-developer specialist profiles
- `2026-06-19 | conventions | minor | no | propagation-opt-in`: add propagation suggestions opt-in to initialization block and convention-levels.md: agents ask once, record preference, surface suggestions at moment of discovery when opted in
- `2026-06-19 | repo | minor | no | contributing`: add CONTRIBUTING.md: design principles, how to propose changes, how to create new role files including the general-as-foundation pattern and role table
- `2026-06-19 | repo | minor | no | readme-wizard`: rewrite README.md as install wizard: action-first quick start with bootstrap prompt, init question list, file manifest, design principles; manual adoption path moved below the fold
- `2026-06-19 | conventions | minor | no | session-signals`: add session-start signal detection and context refresh to session-discipline.md and AGENTS.md: greetings and resumption phrases trigger session-start checklist including instruction file freshness check; context efficiency note (re-read is additive not replacing; new thread preferred when many files changed)
- `2026-06-19 | repo | minor | no | agentics-contributor`: add agentics_contributor flag: set in project memory to enable always-on propagation with agentics repo as explicit candidate; documented in CONTRIBUTING.md agent setup section, convention-levels.md propagation opt-in, and initialization block in root CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md
